- Plan: face recognition over keyframes, named like speakers (#52), feeding covers and the weekly.

  from opencv_zoo, pinned at commit 48b10fa by sha256.
- Spike on kick-131151926 (7,090 keyframes, 22k faces): the two co-hosts one group each across 8 h even
  in costume; PiP faces ~60 px group fine; non-people (game characters, puppets, a wall poster) form
  their own groups, harmless. Cross-stream: the bearded man in a cap matched kick-135522987 (404) at 0.93,
  lookalikes <= 0.55 -> `[faces] match = 0.7` on group centroids. Best frame needs the quarter of a group
  least like it dropped (a cartoon was the top pick), and picks 30 s apart.

## 2026-11-03 (code audit)

- The user asked for a code audit, then an architect's look at usability, and questions. Five read-only
  reviewers by area (core, pipeline, review/publish, services, extras) plus an architecture pass; nothing
  run that calls a model or a service; pytest 281 and ruff green at e4a2bb2. Report:
  `reports/code-audit-2026-11-03.md`. Every 10-28 fix holds (tested). 50 new findings, ranked; the
  pattern is "quietly broken" services (notify loop can die at start, `.bot.lock` makes Health say
  running forever, a `[notify]` typo kills DMs silently, recorder restart loop) and cost guards missing
  on score/cut+thumbs/weekly --render. Architecture: four writers of the shared JSON (#64 is the fix),
  publishing state in three places with no reconcile, the bot is three programs (split `clipper serve`
  out first), three review surfaces for one reviewer, 22 modules untested incl. llm/bot/segments.
  Questions put to the user; no code changed. HANDOFF "Open work" has the summary.

## 2026-11-03 (clipper serve: the split)

- The user's answers to the audit's direction questions, recorded in HANDOFF: NAS direction yes, split the
  jobs loop and the war room out of the bot into `clipper serve`; review in the war room, Discord cards kept
  (approve from the phone); build as if for a package, simple for them first, release in a few weeks; next
  features #84 story, #108 investigate, #109 swarm; #46 second channel still planned; the daily friction is
  switching between Claude and the war room/Discord (#13). "Start the split."
- Branch `serve-split`. `clipper serve` (clipper/serve.py) = the war room thread + the jobs loop moved out of
  bot.py (upload = schedule then upload, preshow, retake, tiktok, x, analytics, cast, news check), each a
  child `clipper` process as before, a non-zero exit queued as a DM. The bot keeps cards, buttons, slash
  commands, DMs and news posting (every 5 min from what serve's check found), and asks serve over the war
  room API (`[serve] url`, the war room token in X-Key) to replan after a decision (`/api/replan`, now with
  `{"sync": true}` for `/schedule replan`) and to start a retake (`/api/retake`, which serve runs on its own
  runner through `warroom.HOOKS`); a serve that is down leaves "not replanned ...; the next hourly run will"
  on the decision instead of failing it (actions.replan `via`). clipper/daemon.py: shared logging, the
  single-instance lock, and a heartbeat `streams/.<name>/status.json` every minute (serve's carries each
  job's running / last run / next due); the Health panel, `clipper services status` and `clipper doctor`
  read them, which closes audit #3 (`.bot.lock` never unlinked) and #2 (tailnet_status outside the notify
  loop's try: the bot now asks serve's /api/health for the link, inside the try). `[warroom] with_bot`
  removed; `[serve] url` and `[paths] serve_log` added (clipper.toml backed up to
  backups/clipper.before_serve_split_2026-11-03.toml). deploy/serve task + supervisor; `clipper services`
  knows serve (`all` = worker, serve, bot); doctor `--role serve` (tailnet, YouTube token, cast OpenCV,
  warroom host/token) and `--role bot` checks serve answers; setup role "serve" (faces extra; the bot's is
  discord only). Tests: daemon, serve (jobs table, due, status, the client through the handler's checks),

## 2026-11-03 The search swarm (#109), first of three PRs for `clipper story` (#84)
- User: "lets start the story command". Split agreed: swarm + helpers, then story phase 1 (to the

  and C:\clipper keeps running the X change live.
- `clipper/swarm.py`: planner (Sonnet, up to `first_tasks` 16) → workers (Haiku, `workers` 8) that run one
  tool in code and answer findings by result number (strong / maybe) plus up to 3 follow-ups → coordinator
  (merge by platform id, seen-set by kind + normalized target, stop at max_rounds 3 / max_tasks 60 /
  budget_usd 1.0 / max_wall_s 600 per run). Trail through jsonstate, resumed on rerun (done kept, failed and
  pending run). Guardrails: targets parse as their kind or are dropped (noted in the trail), a regex drops
  whereabouts / private-detail targets, no downloads. Per-source `[swarm] rates` (kick.RateLimiter).
- Tools: `youtube_search` (results URL with the upload-date `sp` filter for the run's window; yt-dlp
  2026.10.10 has no `ytsearchdate`, and YouTube's sort-by-date `sp` is ignored for flat listings),
  `youtube_channel` (/videos + /streams, `channel_depth` 200, run's dates, then `focus` on the goal's words),
  `kick_videos` (new `kick.list_videos`), `odysee_channel` (`list_odysee(limit=)`), `reddit_search` /
  `web_search` (investigate's Brave helpers), `archive_search` (FTS hits with links at the moment),
  `our_streams`. `youtubetab:approximate_date` gives flat entries a day from "3 days ago".
- `Budget` / `OverBudget` / `estimate` moved from investigate to llm.py (investigate re-imports them) with a
  `spent_before` baseline, since the swarm shares its caller's client.
- Live: each tool once against the real sources (all eight work); one run on "Jer's dog Huck: the MRI he
  raised money for" (30 days, $0.25 cap, max_tasks 30): 16 planned + 14 follow-ups, 109 findings (44 strong:

  ~$0.025. It showed Jer's YouTube channel's 20 newest are all midterms: hence channel_depth + focus; even
  200 reaches back only days for him, so his archive subject is the way into his own older videos.
- 12 tests in tests/test_swarm.py (targets, private targets, finding keys, merge + follow-up rounds, each
  stop condition, resume, seeds/known, settings, rate spacing, window/focus, trail tree); 373 pass, ruff clean.
- PR #133 Copilot round (1 comment, taken): links are stripped from the credit line too. Merged as ca8e57d.

## 2026-11-03 `clipper story` phase 1 (#84): brief → swarm → triage → ingest → moments → dossier

- User: "Start phase 1". Branch `story-84` in worktree `.claude/worktrees/story` (off main 57971de).
- `clipper/story.py`, `clipper story new | run | add | status | trail | show`. A story is the archive
  subject `story-<slug>` plus brief.json, swarm.json, triage.json, moments.json, dossier.json/.md.
  Steps skip when done; `--from` starts at a step and runs everything after it (only those).
- Brief: people from `[paths] streamers` (research_notes/streamers.json, until now unread by code) and
  `[story] people`, matched by name, parenthetical nickname or slug as whole words; `--person` for one
  the brief doesn't name. Their YouTube/Kick/Odysee channels are swarm seeds; Rumble/Twitch are context.
- Triage (Haiku): numbered video findings → ordered picks; code keeps `--max-videos`/`--max-hours`
  (unknown length counts as an hour). Our own streams (a Kick finding whose `kick-<id>` has a transcribed
  `streams/<id>`, or an our_streams segment) are linked through a catalog `dir` (new `archive.item_dir`,
  used by `archive_index.build`, which then keeps the catalog's meta over the stream's). A triage rerun
  replaces picks not yet transcribed.
- Ingest: `archive._transcribe_all`; new Kick branch in `transcribe_item` (yt-dlp `--referer` + `[archive]
  kick_format` "worst" = 160p, ~290 MB for 3 h → extract_audio → flac → `worker.transcribe`). Any failure
  stops the run (a dossier without it reads as the whole story). `transcribe_item` takes `retry_on`;
  `[story] youtube_bot_retry` (default off) adds the bot check to the signed-in retry.
- Find (Haiku): story_expand → FTS hits over the story index and archive subjects the swarm hit (window
  applied there) → story_moments picks with role event/reaction/context → quote located with
  investigate's `locate` near the window, kept in the transcript's words; same-spot picks deduped.
- Dossier (Sonnet medium): summary, timeline (shown / reported / speculation), people, who said what,
  conflicts, coverage, gaps, angles; cites M##/S## must resolve (one retry, then ClipperError). `notes`
  and a source's `struck` survive a rerun; a struck source can't be cited. `story show` re-renders.
- Live: "DSP says his credit score is 590", 7 days, 4 videos / 5 h. Swarm 33 tasks, 92 findings (44 strong),
  $0.027. Triage picked 3 YouTube clips/reactions (DSP's Kick VODs are 3-11 h, over the cap) and linked 5 of
  our streams. All four YouTube downloads failed on the bot check (archive retries only age checks), so
  the dossier came from our streams: 7 moments (1 quote dropped), a sound timeline, the 589/590 and
  "fake letter" conflicts, gaps. Models ~$0.08 total. Kick audio checked separately on 2 min of DSP's 11-04
  VOD (format pick, download 5 s, Modal 263 s with cold start, ~$0.07).
- 10 tests in tests/test_story.py; 387 pass, ruff clean; doctor counts the four story prompts.
- User: "Yes, turn it on" for the signed-in bot-check retry: `[story] youtube_bot_retry` defaults to true.
  The rerun retried all four signed in and was turned away again: the 10-31 cookie export is stale
  (browsers rotate them). Needs a fresh export, then `story run dsp-credit-score-590`.
- PR #135 Copilot round (5 comments, all taken): a hand-given Kick VOD we recorded is linked; each video
  counts once against the limits; find writes an empty moments.json rather than leave an older one; a
  claim citing nothing is sent back; "(transcribed)" only when the words exist. CI: triage's Modal
  estimate no longer needs the modal package. 391 tests.
- Fresh cookie export (user, 11-03 23:02): the rerun transcribed all four signed in; find 6 moments (all
  verified), dossier with DSP's own "pulled my real credit report... completely different" (his video) beside

  videos too ("we need to cross reference"), add X posts as a source for story and investigate, and the

  start` → X reads → vault export, plus a review gate before content, Wayback snapshots, source reliability,
  and later a watchlist, public records, a known-stories test set and keep/skip feedback.

## 2026-11-04 lock race on the NAS share

- `clipper upload` (serve's hourly run) failed: `PermissionError [WinError 5]` on
  `streams/kick-134990485/review.json.lock`. Its holder was deleting the lock just then, and over SMB a
  file that is mid-delete refuses stat/open with Access denied instead of not-found.
  `jsonstate._acquire_file` now treats PermissionError as "still held" and waits; the release unlink
  retries a busy lock instead of raising, then keeps trying from a thread (a waiter's 60 s runs out
  before a lock is stale at 120 s), but only until 10 s before the lock could be taken as stale,
  counted from when it was made. Each lock now holds a random token and release deletes it only if
  it is still ours, so a holder past STALE_S can't delete a successor's lock (true before this PR
  too); the cutoff is checked before every try, so a holder past it leaves the lock to the waiters'
  stale takeover (read-then-delete isn't atomic). A folder that refuses lock creation outright fails
  at the deadline instead of spinning. Everything after the first fix came from Copilot's six
  reviews of PR #148 (approval recommended on the sixth). Seven new tests in tests/test_jsonstate.py.

  project `clipper-hub`: serve, bot, worker, tailscale (userspace; UGOS owns host :443), host networking,

  resolved every entry on both machines; #9 and #12 closed.
- Tailscale sign-in: containerboot's `tailscale up` times out after ~60 s and the container restarts with a new link,
  so the user's first approval landed on a dead one. A one-off tailscaled on the same volumes with `up --timeout=0`

## 2026-11-05: war room "next open slot" (segments wouldn't drag into slots)

- Why the drag failed: every approved segment without a slot was over its stream's
  `stream_budget` (or stale), and the schedule's "Approved, no slot yet" list hid those (`dropped`),
  so there was nothing to drag. The list now shows them, tagged "over budget" / "stale".
- New `/api/slot` and ⇥ buttons (per item, item window, "Segments to open slots…"): the slot comes from
  `schedule.next_free` (slot times, max_per_day, min_gap, kick_first_hours via `release_window`, min lead);
  a preview from a dry replan (`schedule.with_pins`) names entries a pin would bump; then pins through
  actions.py and one replan, undoable. Bulk keeps to `stale_days` and leaves out streams at budget (a pin
  there only swaps). Real data: 1 of 181 segments qualified (Baxter Bulletin, pinned for Sat 11-07 17:00
  from a local war room); the rest are stale or from kick-136068029, which is at its 10 already.

## 2026-11-05 Claude Code on the NAS (#65, branch nas-claude)

- User: "start gh issue #65". Built in a worktree off main (C:\clipper-nas-claude) so the uncommitted
  HANDOFF/log edits on retake-compilation-trim stay put.
- deploy/claude: its own compose project `clipper-claude` (a hub deploy never ends a session), the hub's
  Debian 13 + QSV base plus git, gh and Claude Code's native build (inside the image: a session reads
  its binary from disk, so not the NAS volume; rebuilt at most daily via CLAUDE_CODE_BUILD). The clone
  and Claude's home are bind mounts in /volume1/docker/clipper-claude/{repo,home}; the entrypoint clones
  CLIPPER_REPO with GH_TOKEN, `uv sync`s the clone's venv, waits for `claude auth status`, then
  `claude remote-control --spawn same-dir`.
- Docs (code.claude.com remote-control/authentication): Remote Control needs a claude.ai login and
  refuses to start where ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is set (setup-token tokens can't do it either). So deploy.sh
  turns the key into a compose secret file and clipper got `[paths] anthropic_key`
  (secrets/anthropic_secret.txt) as a fallback, like the other providers. First start asks to enable
  Remote Control and trust /app: `docker attach clipper-claude` once (tty + stdin_open).
- `clipper doctor --role claude`: the worker's checks plus Claude Code installed and signed in (claude.ai),
  no ANTHROPIC_API_KEY / CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN in the environment, the clone on a branch and clean,
  streams writable. CLAUDE.md has a "Running on the NAS" section for that session.
- Not tested in Docker (the laptop's daemon is off) and not deployed; the user needs a GitHub fine-grained
  token (Contents + Pull requests on byvfx/clipper) for GH_TOKEN.

## 2026-11-05: compilation retakes (PR #151)

- User's retake on kick-136068029 `comp-ethan-klein` ("start 10 seconds later, its catching the end of
  a song") came back "not applied": the planner refused every compilation trim.
- PR #151 (merged 11-05 as 0564a6d): a compilation's window is its first segment's start to its last
  segment's end; a trim moves those segments (`comp_edits`, `trim_segments` re-cuts each compilation once)
  and their own items come back for review. Segment/compilation trims snap to word gaps, only the ends
  the note moved; the 3 s minimum is rechecked after snapping (Copilot). `retake_v2` (default now): ends
  can be trimmed, "N seconds later" = nearest line start (the first dry run picked 21564.3, mid-line).
- Ran it from the laptop before the hub cutover: start 21554.3 -> 21562.9 s (cut on keyframe 21559.5, so
  about 8.3 s later than before); comp and s15 at rev 2 waiting for approval, Discord card posted ($0.02).

## 2026-11-07 Chud's face cut off in Shorts (PR #164)

User: Chud's Shorts cut his face off, biased screen right with open space on the left. faces.json
showed why: his cam box is 1.49:1, the Short slot keeps 62% of its width (38% in the cam-only layout),
and his face centre sits at 0.62-0.69 of the box (p90 0.80) with a focus of 0.55 keeping 0.21-0.83.
A fixed focus can't follow him (the median moves between streams), so a panel can now say
`focus = "face"`: `shorts.face_centers` takes the median face centre inside the box during the scene
(the whole stream when the window has none), `panel_crops` centres the window on it, clamped to the
box, and the centres go into the signature. Checked read-only on source frames: all three
kick-136479997 Shorts show his whole face (the clamp leaves his head at the cam's right edge in two,
the cam ends at the frame edge). The NAS write of a stream config override was denied in this
session, so the config flip and re-render wait for the merge and deploy (HANDOFF Open work).
- Follow-up (Mac control station session): Copilot on #164 found the median took the upper middle
  for an even count (now `statistics.median`) and that the fast lane renders Shorts before `faces`,
  so face panels kept the middle on fresh recordings; the worker now runs `shorts` again right after
  `faces` when a layout has a face panel and the Shorts came first (fc647a1). Merged as 91df82e; hub
  deployed by hand from `lofn` (no `deploy/hub/env.local` there, NAS `.env` kept). clipper.toml
  backed up to `clipper.toml.bak-2026-11-07-face`, both Chud cam panels set to `focus = "face"`,
  `clipper shorts kick-136479997` in the worker re-rendered all three (3 rendered, 0 unchanged);
  before/after frames show his whole face in each.

## 2026-11-07 Bootstrapping a Mac as a control station (PRs #165, #166)

- User: "pull down clipper to this machine, I'd like to test the bootstrapping". Fresh Mac (`lofn`):
  no uv, Homebrew ffmpeg. `bash deploy/bootstrap.sh` installed uv 0.13.7 + Python 3.12.16, synced 63
  packages, linked `~/.local/bin/clipper` (already on PATH, `.zshrc` untouched) and started setup.
- Join rejected `~/Downloads/clipper.toml` ("no such file": no `expanduser`). PR #165: `user_path`
  (`~`, surrounding quotes, shell quoting from a dragged-in file; first reading that exists wins),
  the folder holding clipper.toml works, the error names the full path. Copilot: apostrophes in
  shell-quoted paths, `~name` raising RuntimeError, a test that failed on Windows; all fixed.
- Roles had nothing for a machine that only drives the hub. PR #166: `control` role (no extras, no
  transcribe backend, encoder, Modal or Discord token; streams folder optional with `-`; Anthropic key
  and Kitsu token optional, "Kitsu works in the browser"); `doctor --role control` (keys warn, no
  encoder or cookies checks, libass a warning, `online_hub` = ssh + `docker compose ps --services`
  in `[serve] hub`).
- Join copied the share's clipper.toml into the checkout, so `secrets/`, layouts etc. resolved into
  the checkout and doctor couldn't find the war room. Now a config outside the home folder is used in
  place: setup saves CLIPPER_CONFIG and CLIPPER_LOCAL_CONFIG (local file in the checkout, never on the
  share), setup_state.json sits beside the local file, `config.local_path` reads the saved
  CLIPPER_LOCAL_CONFIG on Windows, `bootstrap.sh` reloads both from the profile before restarting
  setup. Control asks for the war room address (tailnet).
- The Mac: old copy kept as `~/clipper/clipper.toml.copy-2026-11-07`; ssh key made, `Host nas` with
  user `Brandon` (case-sensitive on the NAS), `ssh-copy-id` by the user. Doctor 21 ok / 3 warnings /
  0 failures; `clipper services status` showed serve, bot, worker up 20 h and the recorder idle.
- Copilot on #166: the `ffmpeg-full` hint now says to put its bin first on PATH (keg-only); `-` skips
  the streams folder only for control; a mocked test of control's online checks. Fixed in 4506fd1.
  Merged #165 as 6d396d8, then #166 (retargeted to main) as 3e2d6d1. No hub deploy: setup, doctor and
  bootstrap only.

## 2026-11-08 Hand-over audit (reports/code-audit-2026-11-08.md)
- Read-only audit of architecture, maintainability and ease of use, asked as "something I'm proud of
  that others won't have an issue working on later". Metrics over the checkout (ast, ruff, git,
  pytest), two independent fresh-eyes reads (a new maintainer on the package, a new operator on the
  docs, `-h` and the config), every borrowed claim re-checked at the line. No model or service calls.
- Baseline 11-03 -> 11-08: 27,110 -> 34,859 lines, 44 -> 47 commands (+82 sub-subcommands, 272 flags),
  529 -> 626 config keys, 281 -> 546 tests (23 s), 22 -> 21 modules no test names, 13 -> 17 functions
  over McCabe 15, 21 import cycles, HANDOFF 357 -> 539 lines, 506 commits since 10-18.
- Verdict: the core and the executable conventions are sound and both earlier audits' fixes held.
  Risks: growth outrunning tests and docs; the product shaped like this deployment (author's shows
  in DEFAULTS/example, Kitsu required by `clipper run`, no Discord/OAuth/Kitsu setup pages, two deploy
  paths); knowledge in seven places with drift (tests "under a second" = 23 s; Getting-started vs
  Roles-and-services on where services run); four shapes to split (warroom.py, config bookkeeping
  keys, the five inter-process mechanisms, prompt versions by mutating `Prompts`). Five cycles each
  have one wrong-direction edge with a one-hour fix (table in the report).
- Plan: tier 0 a day (Windows test + CI matrix, 77 dead `noqa` + a few ruff rules, 57 merged branches,
  doc drift, test files by subject, `--version`/LICENSE), tier 1 a week (IPC wiki page, cycles,
  warroom split, one JSON helper, prompt registry, tests where the risk is), tier 2 before anyone
  else installs it (scrub the deployment from defaults, setup phase 2 pages, Reviewing page,
  pre-spend estimates, `clipper status`, one deploy path per role, defaults.toml, studio files out).
- Five questions to the user: release audience, a consolidation window, studio files in the repo,
  the Windows task scripts, Kitsu optional. Report and these notes written on the
  docs/install-checklist working tree, uncommitted; nothing else changed.
- Answers (user, same day): both audiences, this deployment first; tiers 0 and 1 before new features
  (~7 days); studio files under `studio/`; Kitsu stays the record, a one-machine Kitsu to document.
  Windows task scripts stay for a GPU worker only (Claude's pick). Recorded in HANDOFF "User decisions".
- Tier 0 the same day, branch `audit/tier-0`, PR #170: `setup._expand` leaves a `~name` with no home
  folder as typed on Windows too (the suite is green here now) and `check.yml` runs Linux + Windows;
  pyproject selects I, B, UP, BLE, RUF100 and C901 at 15 (B905, E731, E741 ignored), 77 stale noqa
  removed, six `raise ... from e`, the 17 functions over 15 marked `# noqa: C901`; CLAUDE.md, Developing,
  Getting-started, Roles-and-services and Install-Windows agree on NAS containers vs Windows task scripts;
  the 10-19 war-room brief to docs/archive/, docs/warroom.md gone; test_audit_fixes*.py refiled into
  test_scoring.py, test_alerts.py and the subject files; `clipper --version`, CHANGELOG.md, launch.json
  relative (preview checked). Waiting on the user: delete the 57 merged remote branches, pick a LICENSE.
- User answers 11-08: merge each tier 1 PR once CI is green and Copilot's comments are handled; LICENSE MIT;
  delete the merged branches (54 remote + locals went). Tier 1 as seven PRs the same day, stacked where they
  touch the same files: #171 process-map page, print/log rule, feature checklist, LICENSE (6 Copilot comments
  on the page's precision, all taken); #172 the five cycles, `notify.publish_and_post`, `digest.py`,
  `weekly.issue_folder`, `states.is_preshow`, heartbeat instead of HTTP (Copilot: resolve the stream's
  [discord] enabled, fixed); #173 `jsonstate.read_or`, `timefmt.now_iso/age_s`, `alerts.save_state` via
  jsonstate, serve `Job`, `actions.EDITS` (the five one-line wrappers gone); #174 `prompts.VERSIONS` +
  `version_of`, 19 `prompts.version =` lines gone, doctor from the table (Copilot: keep the thumbnail-style and
  cast/news conditions, pass the swarm's merged settings; both fixed); #175 `studio/` (a running Fusion held
  `mockups/udder_truth/` so only its tracked files moved; `/mockups/` ignored until merged by hand; Copilot:
  CHANGELOG notes the streamers default); #176 war room split by a generator (13 modules, 104 names
  re-exported, page checked in the preview); #177 tests + module-test rule + `config.base`.
- Tier 2 the same day, five PRs, each merged on green CI (both OSes) with Copilot's comments handled: #178 this
  install out of the defaults and the example (`[receipts]` and `[weekly] masthead`/`byline` empty until set,
  guards in `receipts.find` and `weekly.run`, a doctor warning; the NAS clipper.toml pinned first, backup
  `backups/clipper.before_defaults_scrub_2026-11-08.toml`; a test fails on the install's names in config, example,
  setup and cli; Copilot: blank masthead words → `weekly.style.masthead()`). #179 help on every argument (test),
  `run -h` explains the fast lane's twins, Getting-started stops calling `--limit`/`--from` universal, doctor
  warns on `auto_upload_minutes = 0` and a bot role with Discord off (Copilot: a non-list masthead; `--only`/`--from`
  choose stages, not what they redo). #180 `--dry-run` estimates on score, segments, meta and thumbs
  (`llm.tokens`/`estimate_lines`), per-stream costs from 27 logged streams on Costs (transcribe $0.45, score
  $0.18, segments $0.81, meta $0.88, thumbs $1.01 median; about $3.50 a show), `clipper status` (Copilot ×5:
  prices offline, the Pass A and metadata checkpoints, no worker heartbeat file, review counts without Kitsu via
  `actions.describe(links=False)`). #181 pages Kitsu (+ `deploy/kitsu/local.sh`), Discord app, YouTube sign-in,
  Reviewing; `clipper review invite <discord id>` (`notify.invite`); compose the one way for serve and the bot
  (`deploy/serve` and `deploy/discord` removed, `services.run_task` says so); product pages without this install's
  test stream, NAS address or channel (Copilot ×4: invite writes the file's own list and refuses when a layer
  overrides it, `toml_set` replaces multi-line values whole, status without Kitsu was #180's fix, sign in where
  there is a browser and copy the files to serve). #182 `clipper/defaults.toml` the one source of defaults (the
  migration asserted equality with the old literal; config.py 1,330 → 446 lines), `clipper.example.toml` a 58-line
  starter, the wheel checked to carry the file. Not done: prompts into the package. The hub still runs the
  pre-tier-1 code until `bash deploy/hub/deploy.sh`.
- The user asked for the app's auto-compaction at 350k tokens: not among the Code tab settings reachable from a
  session (auto-archive, branch prefix, notifications, remote control, output style, keep-awake), so told them.
- #182 merged on green CI (fe49329). The hub deployed at 13:14, right after chudlogic's stream (kick-136671441,
  about 09:00 to 13:12) ended and with no serve job running: `bash deploy/hub/deploy.sh` took 80 s; serve, bot
  and worker came up on the new image (626 defaults loaded from `clipper/defaults.toml`), heartbeats fresh.
  Waiting for that stream, the recorder watch fired once on a read of `.record/status.json` that landed
  mid-replace: `clipper record status` said "no heartbeat", exit 0, which `deploy/record/deploy.sh` takes as
  "not live" and would have restarted the recorder mid-show. PR #183: `daemon.read_heartbeat` reads a missing
  or unreadable heartbeat again (three reads, 0.3 s apart) when its folder exists; `daemon.heartbeat` and
  `services.recorder` both use it; How-the-processes-talk names the readers and the retry. Copilot: a file still
  unreadable after the retries must be unknown, not empty, or the guard it was meant to fix opens the other way;
  so it raises, the readers report "unreadable", `record status` exits 1 and stop/restart/deploy refuse without
  `--force`. Five tests.
- PR #184, the last plan item: the prompts ship inside the package. `prompts/` → `clipper/prompts/` beside the
  loader (`clipper/prompts/__init__.py`, `importlib.resources`); `prompts.find(cfg, file)` reads `[paths] prompts`
  first, now optional (`""` → None, `config.OPTIONAL_PATHS`), and the checkout fallback is gone; doctor checks the
  files where they are read from and warns when the folder named isn't there; the Dockerfiles and
  `deploy/hub/deploy.sh` stop copying `prompts/`; a scratch wheel lists the 101 files. The NAS clipper.toml's
  `prompts = "prompts"` (the old example's pinned default, a folder that never existed, hidden by the fallback)
  removed, backup `backups/clipper.before_prompts_key_2026-11-08.toml`. Deployed to the hub at 21:51, in the gap
  after Don's stream (kick-136792024, about 17:30 to 21:50) ended and before the worker started on it: 30 s;
  serve, bot and worker up; the image has the 101 prompt files in site-packages and no /app/prompts or
  CLIPPER_PATHS_PROMPTS; #183's heartbeat retry reached the containers with it.

## 2026-11-09 YouTube cookies for the NAS hub (PR #195)

- User: "we need to update the youtube secrets, i thought this was fixed before". The upload OAuth token was
  fine (refreshed 21:40, 15 uploads that day). The failure: the Kino 11/9 pre-show (9qQAi_81ZM8) hit the bot
  check every 5 min from 21:45, signed-in retry included. The 11-04 fix (`cookies_from_browser =
  "firefox:clipper"`) reads a laptop Firefox profile. The hub containers (11-05) have no browser and leave that
  setting empty, so they fell back to `www.youtube.com_03-11-2026.json`, stale since 11-04.
- The user chose a laptop task and said to note that it prevents fully offline autonomous use. Added
  `clipper cookies export` (`ytdl.export_browser_cookies`: the profile's youtube.com cookies go to
  `[paths] youtube_cookies` as Netscape, tmp + replace, refused when the profile is signed out) and
  `deploy/cookies/cookies-task.ps1` (task `clipper-cookies`, at logon + every 4 h, log `logs\cookies.log`).
  Wiki: Archives, NAS-hub, Roles-and-services. Not yet known: whether the hub's own signed-in use rotates
  the session faster than a 4 h refresh keeps up.
- The user signed the `firefox:clipper` profile in again and ran `clipper cookies export` from the PR branch
  (22:55, 22 cookies); the 23:00 check grabbed the 11/9 pre-show signed in and ran it to review.
- Copilot was out of tokens, so `/code-review` (high) and a fresh review agent stood in. Both found that yt-dlp
  saves its jar back to `--cookies` on exit (non-atomically): the hub wrote the shared file, and a long run
  could undo a fresher export. Fixed in #195: `signed_in_args` gives yt-dlp a private copy in a temp dir
  (JSON exports converted there, so nothing is written beside the shared file); the export skips expired
  cookies, turns an unreachable share into a ClipperError and removes its .tmp; `--to` resolves against the
  config folder; one Netscape writer (`netscape_text`, `read_cookies`); doctor checks the file where no browser
  is set (no live sign-in = FAIL, export over 24 h old = WARN); the hub's bot-check DM points at the laptop
  task; the task logs only the message of a stderr line. Needs a hub deploy.

## 2026-11-10

- #195 merged (CI green on Ubuntu and Windows). Deployed the hub from main (serve, bot, worker recreated);
  the user installed the `clipper-cookies` task on the laptop and its first export ran without errors.
  `secrets/youtube_cookies.netscape.txt` is left on the share for the user to delete.
- #196 merged (`clipper codereview`, the `review` workflow on PRs). Grok's third pass, on 4e2c1b2, raised four
  edge cases left for a follow-up: a reset connection or 408 on a long call is still retried and billed again;
  a binary or mode-only change has the empty digest, so a later one isn't reviewed; `[#70](link to another
  repo)` also posts on this repo's #70; and a review near 65,000 characters loses `</details>` when it is
  kept under a failure (not reachable: `max_tokens` 8000 caps a review near 32k characters). The hub
  doesn't run it, so no deploy.
- Fixed three of those in a follow-up PR: a long call (`timeout_s`) is never sent again after any dropped
  connection or a 408; the digest takes in file headers, modes and a binary file's new blob id; and a
  markdown link's target decides the repo, not its `[#70]` label. The 65,000-character case is left alone.
  Changing the digest means an open PR is reviewed once more on its next push.
- Grok's review of that PR (#197, $0.16, 4.5 min) was one line of plan ("I'll trace the digest…") and no review:
  it wrote as if it could run tools. Prompt `codereview_v2` says it has one reply and no tools, and an answer
  with no `### ` finding and no "No correctness bugs found" is now a failed review (no digest, so the next push
  reviews again). The PR body's "this repo's #5" also put a copy on closed issue #5.
- Copilot's review of #197 (now that extra billing is on) and Grok's second pass found the same gaps: a
  file added on the branch keeps its mode in `new file mode`, which the digest skipped; a link with a
  `"title"` or a root-relative `/byvfx/clipper/issues/N` target lost or misread its number; a link to a
  blob in this repo dropped its `#N` label; and a plan that only quoted "No correctness bugs found" passed
  as a review. Fixed: the digest takes new/deleted file modes, a label goes only when its link names the
  issue or another repo, and a review needs that phrase on a line of its own or a numbered `### ` finding
  with text under it.
- Grok's pass on that fix ($0.87) found two more: `[#70](…/pull/70)` lost 70 (the label went and `URL` read
  only `/issues/`), and a `#N` in a link's title was dropped. `URL` now reads `/pull/N` too and a title is
  kept. Merged on green CI without waiting for another Grok pass, as the user chose.

## 2026-11-10 Copilot to Grok switch

User: "lets put a switch in when i run out of copilot credits it switches to grok for code review". `clipper
codereview --pr` now checks Copilot first (`[codereview] copilot_wait_s`, 600; 0 = always Grok): a real Copilot
review of the head commit means no Grok call; its notice (a review whose body starts "Copilot was unable to
review ... insufficient remaining quota", seen on #191), no request (60 s grace) or no review in the wait all
run Grok, and so does any GitHub error, so a PR is never left unreviewed. Copilot has been requested by hand
only on #194 and #197 of the PRs checked (#194-#212), and there is no ruleset, so until automatic review is on
at GitHub every code PR goes to Grok after the minute's grace. Not seen live: how REST lists Copilot in
`requested_reviewers` (matched on "copilot" in the login; if that misses, Grok runs as well, about $1).

Then the user: "default i want copilot", on PRs only. Added the repo ruleset "Copilot reviews new PRs" (id
27014216: `copilot_code_review`, `review_on_push` false, no drafts), so later pushes go to Grok. CLAUDE.md,
HANDOFF and Developing no longer say Copilot is paused. Grok's review at 1c915ab raised two narrow cases left
as they are: `copilot_stands` doesn't compare commits, so dismissing only the newest of several Copilot
reviews keeps a note in force; and a linked issue left on a note after a failed post isn't given later findings.
Copilot then reviewed #213 itself (requested 4 s after a push, as byvfx: the new ruleset or by hand, not
known) and found a review mentioning "quota" read as its notice; now only an opening "Copilot was unable /
could not ..." counts. Grok's last pass (4701d46) added one more narrow case, left: a merge of main during
the 10-minute wait, before the note is posted, is reviewed again (about $0.50). Merged 11-11.

## 2026-11-12 B-roll library (#229, step 1)

The user asked what besides DVIDS gives free government video for b-roll; the answer became #229 and
then step one: `clipper broll` (clipper/broll.py, wiki B-roll). Three sources with no key: NASA's
images-api (search, then each item's collection.json for the ~orig.mp4 and metadata.json for the
length), the Internet Archive's Prelinger collection (advancedsearch, then /metadata/<id> for the mp4
and `licenseurl`), and Commons video (the cast library's API with `filetype:video`). A clip is kept
only for a licence in `[broll] licences` (pd, cc0) and no outside owner named in its text (©,
copyright, Getty, AP, Reuters; "not subject to copyright" doesn't count). Sound is off by default
because NASA videos often carry licensed music. Over `[broll] max_s` (180 s) an item is listed as
`long`, and `add <id> --from --to` takes a part: ffmpeg seeks over HTTP, so a 20 s part of a 21 min
Prelinger newsreel took 4 s. A live `find "hurricane" -n 1` added one clip per source in 37 s.
Times use parse_time: `1:20` is an hour and twenty minutes, so messages say `0:01:20`.

Step 2 the same day: DVIDS. The user put the keys in `secrets/dvids_secret.txt` on the share (public
key first, private second); only the public key is used, as `api_key` on search and asset calls, from
`$DVIDS_API_KEY` or that file's first line (`[paths] dvids_key`). Search is the B-Roll category
(`[broll] dvids_category`), which has a duration per result; the asset record lists the files, keyless
CloudFront mp4s from 4K to 144p, and the smallest at least `[broll] max_height` tall is taken. The
search results' `hls_url` carries the key, so nothing from them but the fields named is kept. A live
`find "helicopter" --source dvids -n 2` added two clips in 23 s, credits like "SPC ..., 25th Infantry
Division (Army), via DVIDS", and broll.json has no key in it.

## 2026-11-12 Chud overnight test (pins, no code)
The user: "we have alot of chud that is over budget, lets push the rest to private then just drop
the either 30min or 1 hour on top of our other stuff". Chud's segment budget is 0 since 11-10, so
71 approved segments (36 from the 11-09 and 11-10 streams, 35 already stale from 11-06 to 11-08) and
15 compilations were out. The user picked: all 71 segments (no compilations: the same footage
joined), hourly overnight 23:00-07:00 (the hours nothing else publishes, daytime in the UK), captions
later.

Done with what exists, no code change: `actions.pin` on each item (Kitsu Publish at, who "overnight
test"), newest streams first by chat heat, then the stale ones newest stream first: Fri 11-13 00:00
through Fri 11-20 07:00, 8 the first night and 9 a night after. A pin beats both the budget and
stale_days. In `clipper.toml` (backup `backups/clipper.toml.before_chud_overnight_2026-11-12`):
`[schedule] max_per_day` 15 -> 24 (pins count toward it, so 15 would have left the regular slots
unfilled), `[youtube] max_per_24h` 15 -> 26, and a new `[shows.chud.youtube] captions = false`.
serve restarted for the in-process replans; its start-up run placed all 71 and uploaded 11 in 8
minutes (24 of 26 in 24 h, quota 6,064 of 10,000), each private with its publishAt, cover and both
playlists set (checked with `youtube.privacy`). A dry `schedule.with_pins` first showed 71 new, 0
moved, 0 lost.

What limits this is the Data API quota, not the upload count: uploads have their own bucket (100 a
day), but captions are 400 units each, a regular day already spends 4,300-7,400 of the 10,000, so
about 7 extra captioned segments a day fit. Without captions a segment is ~150 (cover + two
playlists). `backfill_captions` only retries entries with `captions_error`, so these need that flag
set by hand once the table is removed. `ytquota.spare` reserves `upload_units` (600) for every
upload the cap still allows, so with the cap at 26 the link and caption backfills only get quota
late in the day. There is no lock between two `clipper upload` runs: the hub's hourly job did all
of it, nothing was uploaded from the laptop.

2026-11-13: the user said yes to a scheduled wrap-up. Task `chud-overnight-wrapup` (one-off, Fri 11-20 09:00,
in the desktop app) checks the 71 went out, puts both caps back to 15, removes `[shows.chud.youtube]`, flags
the 71 for the caption backfill, restarts serve and reports. The pin list is kept at
`backups/chud_overnight_pins_2026-11-12.json` on the NAS. 14 of the 71 were up by 11:40 today.

## 2026-11-13: `clipper doc`, the mini-documentary pipeline

The first mini-documentary was built by about 145 one-off Resolve scripts, each patched from the last.
`clipper doc` is that method as a command (design agreed with the user first: the name, a TOML plan, the
leak check on Modal, and `[story] lufs` so stories can target -14 too).

- `plan.toml` replaces the hard-coded Python plan: `[[seq]]` entries (section, card, clip, say), cover
  shots cued on a line's own words, checked when the plan is read, so a bad cue fails before narration is
  bought. `say` lines sharing a `take` are one read, cut in the pause before each line.
- Word times come from a small local Whisper, aligned to the script (`doc.align`), not searched in the
  heard words: a number read out ("800") no longer breaks a cue. faster-whisper's own file reader fails
  with this PyAV, so the audio is decoded by ffmpeg, as the old scripts did.
- The layout is the old plan loop, with its special cases as plan options (`hard_out`, `mute`, `silent`,
  `sound`, `note`). One fix on the way: a take cut in two was a frame short or long at the cut, because
  the block's length and the sound's were rounded separately.
- Levels are worked out before Resolve (`set_levels`: clips and takes to `[doc] dialogue_lufs`, music and
  room tone under it), so the Resolve step only places rows, sets gains and fades, and reads back.
- `master`: gain plus a limiter, picture copied. On a 45 s excerpt of a real render: -15.9 -> -14.2 LUFS.
  The limiter sits 0.8 dB under `[doc] true_peak`: at the ceiling itself the AAC encode peaked 0.6 dB over.
- `leak`: banned terms against the render's Modal transcript and kit.md; exact, joined or split, and
  close spellings (`[doc] leak_fuzzy`); `!phrase` in banned.txt marks a false alarm.
- Tried on real files (free): take processing (17.0 -> 15.9 s), word timing (96% of script words
  matched), the split, mastering. Not tried: Resolve (a stand-in in `tests/test_doc_resolve.py`),
  ElevenLabs, Modal. 759 tests pass.

Later the same day, Copilot's review of #233 (12 of 13 fixed: the project's frame rate checked before
placing, a refused gain fails the build, mutes as a union, no music under a silent stretch, caches keyed
by path, size and mtime, the leak matcher's joined names and possessives, a short last chapter, a story
part's render named for its loudness) and a run against live Resolve 21.2 with a synthetic 28 s piece:
- `AppendToTimeline`'s `endFrame` is exclusive, as the old scripts assumed (Copilot said inclusive): every
  item read back at `end - start` frames.
- A still (a PNG label) ignores the range it is asked for and lands at Resolve's default five seconds, so
  the next one is pushed along: `still_movies` turns each still into a qtrle movie long enough for its
  longest use. The old scripts never met this because their overlays were already .mov files.
- Gains (`AudioVolume`) and `SetFades` read back as set; the render was 839 frames as planned, and the
  master went -16.0 -> -14.0 LUFS.

Main's new review rule (Grok's workflow gone; `code-review` at high effort on the branch) arrived with
the merge of main, so the branch got that review too. Ten findings, all fixed: the leak matcher now ends
a word at a hyphen, slash or dash and counts a plural (`Kent-based`, `the Kents`); a short line between
two clips keeps its two fades apart; a muted clip's pieces share one gain; a story's loudness is always
in its signature and its parts' names (so a story cut before `[story] lufs` is rendered again whatever
the setting); a reel at another rate has its sound rows converted like its picture (untried against
Resolve); a music cue at a section nothing follows, a fractional `fps` and a missing `[media]` file are
one-line errors; music and room tone are never boosted past +14 dB or when nothing measures; loudness
is cached in `work/loudness.json` (the synthetic piece rebuilt in 0.5 s instead of 1.2 s).

## 2026-11-14 Review pre-flight checks (#259 step A)

- Built `clipper review check` (`clipper/review_check.py`, wiki Review#pre-flight-checks), run by
  `review publish` first (`[review] checks`). Four checks per item, findings in the stream's
  `checks.json` (keyed by a signature of what was read, so nothing is checked or paid for twice):
  text (`[checkup] words` + `actions.style_issues`), a Short's webcam panels (8 frames of the
  rendered Short through YuNet; "cut off" is an eye, the nose or the mouth past the panel edge, since
  a box against the edge was only PPP leaning in), the cover (the known face in its box against
  `main_person`, then one Sonnet vision call), the subject (a person the title names whom the
  transcript never does; a segment or compilation whose first 15 minutes name someone else 3+ times
  and its subject never). Shown in a Kitsu Checks column, the Discord card, the war room badge and
  window, and `clipper status`.
- Webcam panels are marked `cam = true` per layout panel (or `focus = "face"`); the mark is left out
  of the Short render signature so adding it re-renders nothing. Not set on the NAS yet: the deployed
  code would count it. A faces.json "is this box usually a face" guess was tried and dropped (Kino's
  cams scene sits inside the watch scene's video box).
- Backtest without vision on the nine reject streams: caught the two Chud no-face Shorts, the Jerry
  Banfield/Jer title and comp-angry-joe's DSP opening (DSP 7 mentions in 15 min, "Joe" none). The
  vision prompt, tried on three covers (~5 cents in all), catches the iDubbbz cut-off face and the
  wrong Brandon Herrera side photo and passes an approved cover, once told the tabloid clutter is the
  house style. The three taste rejects (too much Jer, Chimpwrecked, Chud gaming) are for the grace
  window. A PPP in blue face paint reads as "no face": costumes are a known miss.
- Backtest over every stream (45 streams, 780 approved, no vision): 86 approved items flagged, 67 of them text
  only (old "hosts" descriptions, real by the rule), 19 picture/subject/cover (2.4%). The first run had 16
  cover flags that were name gaps (`main_person` is free text: "Jeremy Hambly", "ReviewTech USA", "Kino's
  chef"); the face check now flags only two different people.json records, which leaves 1.
- Vision backtest at the user's word (808 covers, $8.89; ~$9.45 with the retries): both cover rejects
  caught; 67 of 780 approved covers flagged: 13 problems that look real (black frames; the same wrong Herrera
  and "Ryan Kinel" side photos on several covers), 33 "not the person" (mostly Andy's fox suit), 32 cut-off
  faces you approved anyway. A costume line in the prompt clears the fox suits, but worded "even when the
  face differs" it also hid the Herrera side photo (4 of 4 runs): the final wording is narrower and keeps it
  (3 of 3). Tried on 9 covers, not rerun over all.
- Local code review at high: 9 findings, all fixed (publish survives a check crash, signature covers
  people/faces/layouts/segment, images shrunk before the call, per-field host dedupe, only checked
  items written, names indexed once, doctor warns when a show marks no webcam, wiring tests).
- PR #263 merged and deployed (Copilot never reviewed it: quota; Grok's `clipper codereview`, $0.88, found 5, all
  fixed). The webcam panels are marked `cam = true` in clipper.toml (Kino watch 2-3, watch_hosts, cams; Channel
  404 watch's Don, desk); doctor's warning is gone.

## 2026-11-14 Approval by silence (#259 step B)

- `clipper review auto` (`clipper/review_auto.py`): an item approves itself once its show has `[review]
  auto_approve`, nobody acted on it, its checks are clean, its score is at least `auto_min_<kind>` (short 6,
  the rest 0 = by hand) and it has been in review `auto_grace_minutes` (120, from the new `published_at` in
  review_state.json). Pre-shows at publish (`auto_preshow = "now"`); stories and one-offs by hand. Every
  person action in actions.py marks `auto.json` first (after its own input checks), and a decision seen in
  Kitsu does too, so a reopened item stays with a person. serve runs it every 10 min; it syncs and re-checks
  what is due, re-reads auto.json before each approval, and replans once. Card field "Auto-approve" and the
  war room's item window say when, or why not; checkup's new `auto` check and the audit count the ones a
  person undid. A new revision takes an automatic approval back.
- Flat `auto_*` keys rather than a `[review.auto_approve]` table: a show's override replaces a whole key, so
  a nested table would lose its defaults.
- Backtest on the past Shorts (last session's check results, vision included): 105 of 181 approved would
  have approved themselves (Kino 60/117, the rest mostly old "hosts" text; c404 42/54; Chud 3/10), and 1 of
  4 rejected ("too much Jer").
- Local code review at high: 9 findings, all fixed (republish, a person acting mid-run, a crash per stream,
  touch after validation, describe's re-reads, the deadline before the first pass, text floors, the wiki).

## 2026-11-15: approval by silence hardening (#259)

- The leftovers from PR #270's review, one branch (`review-auto-hardening`): the approval held
  auto.json's lock over open-ended Kitsu calls (gazu never times out), so a stall over 60 s could leave
  a person's action unrecorded and over 120 s let another process take the lock. Now
  `review.kitsu_deadline(25)` bounds each Kitsu request of the decision (this thread only), the item is
  re-read after the lock goes (a failed re-read mirrors "approved" in review.json so the replan places
  it), and `claimed_at` marks an approval whose answer was lost: if Kitsu took it, the next pass stamps
  `approved_at` rather than "approved by a person".
- Every check run but a publish's (the recheck, the approval's own run, `clipper review check`) writes
  the Kitsu Checks column (`review.set_checks`, kept as the proposal in review_state.json); sync never
  counts that column as an edit (`review.ADVISORY`). Shows without auto get the hourly recheck too while
  serve's auto job runs; items no longer in review.items are skipped until their checks.json entry
  changes (`recheck.json` "gone").
- Local code review (high): 9 findings, 6 fixed; skipped: the deadline is per connect/read (a trickling
  response isn't cut; Kitsu's answers are small), the non-auto recheck's vision spend (only items whose
  inputs changed, ~1 cent each), and a person approving in Kitsu's own page after a lost answer (rare).
  1021 tests, ruff clean.
- PR #273. Copilot's 3 findings fixed (e2e5829): the mirror writes "approved" only while auto.json
  still says approved and review.json is still pending; set_checks keeps what Kitsu took when a later
  write fails; review check sends Kitsu what checks.json kept (a newer run's result wins). 1024 tests.
  Merged and deployed 18:27, while Channel 404 was recording (the hub deploy never restarts the recorder;
  it kept recording).

## 2026-11-16: the Clippa messenger, v1 (#158; branch chat/messenger-v1)

Built from the 11-15 spec comment on #158 in a side session; not deployed.
- `clipper/catalog.py`: the allowlist Clippa can use, each tool tagged read / write / spend. Writes are
  built from cli.py's own parsers (approve, reject, retake, reopen, hold/release, pin/unpin, retitle,
  cover text) plus slot; they go through `actions.decide` as "Clippa chat", replan, and land in the war
  room's undo list as "(chat)". Spends (story, cover redo) show an `llm.estimate` first. A test keeps
  upload, schedule, config, services, the recorder and the rest out.
- `clipper/chat.py`: local matching first; otherwise one Haiku call through the new `LLM.tool_call`
  (Claude tool use, and function tools on OpenAI-compatible servers), ledger label `chat`, capped by
  `[chat] daily_usd`. Topics (`/new`, ×, 2 h quiet) end with a receipt; open cards expire with their
  topic and re-check the item on tap. Jobs post back into the chat; `/away` holds them. `/clear`
  archives to `chats/archive/`.
- The page is `/chat` on serve's server: our own bevelled-window IM look, a buddy list from the
  heartbeats, "Clippa is typing…", folds for phones. The war room is untouched.
- Live test on `kick-131151926` (laptop, a test warroom on :8767 with a scratch chats folder): "what goes
  out tomorrow" answered from schedule.json; "can you pin the v05 short from kick-131151926 for november
  30th at 9am" took one Haiku call ($0.0005) that picked pin, and Yes wrote Publish at 11-30 09:00 to
  Kitsu and a pinned entry to schedule.json; "unpin v05 kick-131151926" put it back. A vague item name
  ("the blade in vegas segment") got a "Which one?" with three buttons.
- Fix on the way: a named item searched every stream (40 s after a replan); with a stream id in the
  message only that stream is read (under 1 s).
- Local code-review (high) found 9, all fixed: a tool error rolled back the message, "fri 3" read as 03:00 and
  today's weekday as next week, "approve s02 from kick-..." put the stream in the note, a card left "working" by
  a restart, the chat lock held through the model call and Kitsu, a cut-off tool pick, story argv values read
  as options (now --opt=value and "--"), two-letter section aliases, the overflow trim cutting the open topic.
- Looks (user: "id like to tool this so that we can switch between themes", then "Yes, all four" after a
  mockup): structure in `chat.css` reads only custom properties; each look is `web/themes/<name>.css`
  (retro, millennium, pager, warroom), picked per browser under the buddy list, default `[chat] theme`.
  serve lists the folder (`/api/chat/themes`), so a new look is a new file. Window titles are the
  theme's `--label-*`. Tests: every theme defines every property; warroom keeps warroom.css's palette;
  no real messenger's name in the page.
- Copilot's review (8 inline): same-second archive names, a retitle undo that couldn't undo, slot signed as
  the war room, a cover estimate that left out face frames and the retry, a spend card not re-checking its
  item, the daily cap blind to calls in flight, a late answer landing in a new topic; all fixed in 0285d72.
  Merged at the user's word ("merge"); not deployed.

- Before recording the user asked whether Twitch ingest existed. It didn't (`clipper fetch` was Kick only; Twitch
  was only a label in `people`/`story`), so #292 was filed, then built the same session and YouTube added to it.
  `clipper fetch` picks the source from the URL: `clipper/twitch.py` and `clipper/ytvod.py`, stream ids
  `twitch-<id>` and `youtube-<id>`, settings `[twitch]`, `[youtube] fetch_max_height`, `[paths] twitch_cookies`;
  wiki page Fetching-VODs; `clipper doctor` checks the cookies file.
- Twitch chat: yt-dlp has none, so it is Twitch's web query (the player's client id and persisted-query hash, which
  Twitch can change). Cursor pages fail the integrity check, so it pages by `contentOffsetSeconds`; a second with
  more than one page of messages keeps only its first page. Progress goes to `chat.partial.jsonl`, and a rerun resumes.
  A vanillamace VOD (3.4 h) gave 39,838 messages in about 5 min; her 3.5 min VOD fetched whole (1080p, 161 MB, 1,164 messages).
- YouTube chat is yt-dlp's `live_chat` replay: Don's 4.07 h stream gave 8,671 messages in 40 s (no video downloaded).
- Reviews: my high-effort review found a crash on a relative cookies path (no `paths.root`), lost chat on a mid-run
  failure, a swallowed chat error and others, all fixed. Grok (`clipper codereview`, $0.465; the key is read from
  `secrets/openrouter_secret.txt`, not the env) found the chat-sign-in gap and a bad resume line, fixed. Then
  trying the cookies found the export is JSON, which yt-dlp rejects: it now gets a private Netscape copy.
- Tried: kurlyfox (no public VODs), `channel404news` (simulcasts the YouTube stream, no VODs), `nmnader` (none left).
  The Windows check failed once on `test_kitsu_deadline_bounds_every_request_then_lets_go` (20.00000000000003 > 20,
  unrelated float flake); a rerun passed. Merged on green CI at the user's word, before Copilot reviewed. Not deployed.
- HANDOFF and this entry are uncommitted on main.

  tooltip's shadow blur (20 to 30 px). The theme's `color-scheme: dark` would have turned the war room's native

  tabs' last one off screen. The row scrolls itself now (`.tools` overflow-x on phones).
- Tests: every look defines every property both structures read; warroom.css has no `:root` and only theme-free
  colour literals (black behind video, the dark badges over covers); both pages load the look after their structure

## 2026-11-16: Clippa renames videos that are up

- The user: Clippa "really need[s] to be the point of control", starting with renaming an already
  published video. Before this a retitle of an uploaded item changed Kitsu's column only; YouTube kept the
  old title and nothing said so.
- `youtube.retitle` reads the video's snippet, swaps the title (show name and bleeps as `metadata` does) and
  writes the rest back as YouTube has it; 51 units, from `ytquota.spare` only; a video deleted in Studio is
  marked `gone_at`; one YouTube already has under that title skips the 50-unit write.
- First cut (PR #291) put it in a new `actions.retitle`, so only the chat and the CLI renamed. The user
  then: "make the war room title edits rename on youtube too". So it moved into `actions.edit_meta`, which
  every title edit goes through (war room item window and find and replace, the bot's details form, the
  chat, `clipper review retitle`, undo): YouTube first, then the Kitsu columns, so a failed rename changes
  nothing and a Kitsu failure after it says the public part happened. A video deleted in Studio: Kitsu only.
- The chat card says **Rename s02 on YouTube?** with the link and the title as YouTube shows it; no spare
  quota or a deleted video is an answer, not a card. Kitsu's own title still makes a card on a video
  that's up (Studio may have renamed it). The war room asks before saving a new title for a live item,
  marks the live renames in find and replace's preview, and its undo note stops saying "change it in
  Studio" for title-only edits; the bot's form labels the title "(renames the live video)".
- Review of that push: a retake's planned title also goes through `edit_meta`, so the rename is opt-in
  (`rename_live`, set by `decide`, find and replace and undo; never by `retake.py`). Undo renames back on
  YouTube only when the edit was made on a live video. Left: a find and replace over many live titles can
  run out of spare quota partway (each failure is listed).
  Chat prompt v2 (one line: renaming a video that's up is retitle; the rest is Studio).
- Copilot never reviewed (not requested by the ruleset, and two requests by hand didn't register; nothing since
  #285), so Grok (`clipper codereview`, $0.652) at the user's word. Its 3, all real, fixed: a retry after a Kitsu
  failure on the last spare units was refused for quota (now it finishes Kitsu's half when the schedule says
  YouTube has that title already); a chat tap on a video deleted since the card said "Renamed on YouTube" and
  edited Kitsu (now nothing done); undo's "(titles were renamed back there)" counted titles of items not up.

## 2026-11-16: a stream's heat graph in the war room (branch warroom/heat-graph)

- The user, planning the demo video: "can we visually see the heat graph of a stream in the war room?" It couldn't:
  `clipper signals` wrote the curve and nothing drew it. Proposed as its own session, then built in the demo
  session at the user's word, in its own worktree off main.
- `/api/heat?stream=` (`clipper/warroom/heat.py`): `signals.parquet`'s heat cut to about 1,200 buckets, each its
  hottest second (a mean flattens a ten-second peak), None where no chat was recorded; `windows.json`'s windows;
  and each cut item's place in the stream from `review.items` (a segment from `cut_start_s`, a Short or clip from
  its in and out), joined to the queue's cached rows for its state. The curve and spans are cached by the stream
  folder's signature like the rows. About 3 s the first time from the laptop over the share, then instant.
- The page (`web/js/heat.js`): an SVG drawn at the window's width, 70 px an hour at least, so a 12 h stream scrolls
  sideways on a phone. One tip for the whole graph (time, heat, the window's scores, the items there); items are
  buttons that open their window; the table of the twelve hottest windows says what was cut from each or "nothing
  cut here". An approved item the schedule left out says "no slot" and why.
- My review (high effort) found seven things, all fixed before the push: clicking the open item's own span threw
  away unsaved edits; the graph closed before knowing the item could open; the tip showed for a scrolled-away
  part; one sidecar without in/out failed the whole window; window numbers unescaped; dead parameters; dropped
  items looked the same as approved ones.
- Checked with `clipper warroom` from the worktree on port 8767 (the page alone, prewarm off, nothing clicked that
  writes) against Kino 11-14 (12.2 h, 35 items), the test stream and a YouTube VOD with no signals; at phone width
  too. Signals that ran before the transcript have no "said at the peak", so that column hides.
- Not done: no screenshot in the wiki (the product pages don't name this install's shows); Copilot's review (it has
  not reviewed a PR since #285); the hub deploy.
- Main then gained the war room's looks (#290), which conflicted in `warroom.css` and here. Both kept. The graph's
  colours moved onto the look's variables: tints are a look colour at an opacity (no literal colour, which a test
  now refuses), the graph sits on `--field`, and the three light looks got a `--violet` for "uploaded". Looked at
  in the pager and war room looks.
- Grok's review of #294 at the user's word (`clipper codereview`, $0.823): two findings. Real: a window in
  `windows.json` without a numeric heat crashed the page's `toFixed` and hid the curve; the server now leaves out
  a window without its place or heat and gives one without a rank its place in the list, and the tip shows "?"
  for a missing score. Wrong: "uploaded has no colour in the default look"; `themes/warroom.css` defines
  `--violet` (line 12), it saw only the three looks the diff added it to.
- Both merged 11-16 at the user's word, each on green CI with Grok's findings handled: #289 (recorded takes;
  tried against that day's main first, 1201 passed) and #294 (the heat graph; rerun in a browser after the last
  fix). Neither needs the other. #294 is not deployed; #289 needs no deploy, only a pull of main on the laptop.

## 2026-11-17: the demo video's intro (a TV in a dark room), and its kit for Fusion

- The user wanted an opening under the first take ("Hey, I'm Brandon ... I call it Clippa ...", about 25 s). Five
  roughs, each rendered by a scratch script (frames from Python into ffmpeg) and checked from contact sheets and
  audio levels, never by playback. Rough 5 is the one: dark room, a console TV comes on at 1.2 s, a second of
  static, then 41 channels of real clips, each shorter than the last (1.6 s, then x0.92 down to 0.1 s), while the
  camera pushes in until the picture fills the frame (12.5 s); black comes up everywhere but the letters of
  "Clippa" (12-15 s) and the clips run on through them; the set cuts off at 0:20 ("Keep cutoff at 0:20"), plain
  letters to 0:25. Dropped on the way: a bright flash, an Outer Limits section. The clips are picked by machine
  from loud moments (heat 0.5 and up); the user: "I think this is good for now".
- The user: "I really want to get this photo real looking with a camera move", finished by them in Fusion and
  Resolve. So Runway makes a moving plate of the set showing static, and the screen is replaced in post. The kit
  (`mockups/demo-intro/kit/`, gitignored, laptop only): the plate, the channels as one flat 4:3 clip on the
  rough's clock, a soft copy of it and a table of gains for the light on the bezel and the carpet, the title
  matte, two sound stems, seven generated set sounds, the times; clips, light and times at 24 and 30 fps (Runway
  video is 24). `kit5.py` re-makes it from `mockups/demo-intro/work/` (clips, stills, scripts, copied out of
  the session's temp folder).
- The prop: foil rabbit ears on the set, from two photos of the user's. My first try pasted them onto the
  approved still; the user: "generate the antenna with the tv, that way we can keep it with the prop", and the
  only light may come from the screen ("the wall is getting alot of light"). Runway generated the set with its
  antenna in one image but left a glow on the wall in three tries (20 to 36 against 6 in the corners), so
  `wall.py` subtracts the wall's excess light on that native still (walls about 6 everywhere, set and carpet
  untouched).
- Camera move: take 1 (12 s, no antenna) stopped with the tube at 47% of the frame's width. Take 2 from the
  antenna still (15 s, 720p, 450 credits): the walls stay at about 6 for the whole move without a grade, the
  antenna holds until it leaves the frame, the tube's left, top and right edges stay within 2 px of a smooth
  path (`measure_plate.py`; the bottom edge up to 17 px, the bezel's glow), the static's colour is steady, and
  it ends with the screen at 60% of the frame's width and 88% of its height. Runway draws a wider silver
  surround on the bezel as the camera nears. The user: "Use this take", and they are making the Resolve project.
- Resolve: the user made the project `clippa_intro` (24 fps, 1080p). The kit and rough 5 were copied to
  `demo-intro/` on the NAS share (the user's `W:\clipper\demo-intro`; they had said `W:\docker\clipper`, which
  doesn't exist: W: is the `docker` share itself) and imported through the Resolve MCP into bins Plate, Screen,
  Sound and Reference. Timeline `intro_v1`: V1 the plate at its own speed (zoom 1.009 to fill 16:9), V2 the
  screen clip (to pin into the tube), V3 its light, V4 the title matte and V5 rough 5, the last three switched
  off; A1 the clips' sound, A2 the placeholder set sound; seven markers on the rough's beats. The plate ends at
  15 s where the rough's push ends at 12.5 s: retiming it (120%) is the user's call. A still ignores the
  length the script asks for, so the matte sits at 12-17 s and wants dragging out to 25 s.
- The user played `intro_v1` and saw the clip over the plate, not in the set ("needs to be relinked?"). Nothing
  was offline: the pin wasn't built. Built it through the Resolve MCP in a copy, `intro_v2_comp`: plate and
  screen clip made one Fusion clip (`CreateFusionClip`; its comp is reached through the Fusion page's current
  comp, not the item). `ScreenPin` is a Transform whose centre and size are sixth-degree curves fitted to the
  tube's box (measured at a threshold of 150 on blurred frames: every edge within 1.6 px; the first track's
  noisy bottom edge was the bezel's glow at 110), 5% over; `TubeMatte` is the plate's static keyed on
  luminance (0.5 to 0.62); `RoomLit` multiplies the plate, its blue cast taken out, by the picture's mean
  (resized to one pixel, gain 3.04, half saturation); `BlackComesUp` dissolves to the clips through the
  letters over 12-15 s (the matte through a Loader) and `PlainLetters` brings white letters up at 20 s.
  Checked on nine stills through `get_image`, never played. The first stills after the build came back as the
  bare plate from a cache; rewiring the output and back cleared it.
- The user on that first comp: "look at intro rough v5 i like the way the cross fade works there right now its
  comes too early and doesnt feel seamless". Rough 5 never cross-fades two pictures: the push ends with the
  tube 1.06 frames wide at 12.5 s, then everything outside the letters fades to black over the same picture
  (inside lifted a little). The comp now does that: `PlateTime` (a TimeStretcher, the plate's 361 frames
  eased over 0-12.5 s and held), `PlatePush` (the extra 1.78x, eased the same way, moving the tube to the
  middle), the pin's expressions carrying the same push so the picture is resampled once, `BlackComesUp` a
  black merge through the inverted letters, `LettersLift`, `PlainLetters` in the rough's text colour.
- Then: "intergrate the footage better into the crt, need some blur scanlines and grading". On the pinned
  picture: `CRTGrade` (gain 0.94, lift 0.035, saturation 0.9), `CRTSoft` (blur 1.6), `CRTScanlines` (a Custom
  tool, every fourth output row 22% darker; `fmod` and `if` gave black there, `frac` and a comparison work),
  `CRTShaded` (times the plate's blurred static, so the tube's falloff and highlight come back) and `Bloom`
  (the lit screen blurred 30, 0.3, added with a merge whose alpha gain is 0). The multiply made the picture
  layer opaque, so the static key let black through on the bright carpet: `PinArea` (the pin's alpha) now
  limits `TubeMatte`.
- Then: "lets apply the distortion to the clippa text as well then we can animating the distortion flat and the
  crt look gone". There was no geometric distortion yet, so both got one: `PicBulge` and `TitleBulge` are
  Custom tools that sample the image further out with the radius (`getr1b`, strength 0.10 on the picture in
  the tube's space, 0.18 on the letters' matte in the frame's; the pin's overscan went from 5% to 8%). The
  letters also get a soft edge (`TitleSoft`), scanlines on the white (`WhiteLines`) and a glow (`TitleGlow`),
  and one eased ramp, frames 490 to 526 (20.4 to 21.9 s), takes all four to zero. The word is about 6% wider
  while bulged and settles as it flattens.
- The user: the flatten "looks right", and a close-up photo of a CRT for the look (phosphor triads, lines that
  bright areas bloom across, colour smear), then "the footage will need a slight vignette in the tv as well".
  `CRTScanlines` became `CRTPhosphor`: scanlines and red, green and blue stripes anchored to the tube, never
  finer than 3 px on the output and locked to the tube (240 lines, 300 triads) once it is big enough, so the
  pattern is a fine grain far away (16% and 10%) and the close-up's grid when the picture fills the frame
  (42% and 30%, about 7 px a line); red and blue sampled 0.0007 of the width apart; `CRTSoft` blurs 2.4 across
  and 0.9 down; `CRTGrade` now adds colour (saturation 1.18, lift 0.02). `WhiteLines` uses the same pattern at
  the push's end state and loses it on the flatten ramp. The vignette is in `PicBulge` (14% at the edges'
  middles, twice that in the corners). The Custom tool's `cos` did nothing, so the pattern is `frac` and `abs`.
- The user: "we need to match blacks, lets bring the blacks up ... like a lift in nuke the footage should have
  a more washed out look right now the values dont make sense". Measured on a rendered frame, the footage's
  black was 11 of 255 against 56 on the bezel and 19 on the cabinet beside it: a lit tube darker than the wood
  it lights. `CRTGrade` now lifts 0.12 (0.17 put it at 70, above the bezel), with gain 0.96 and the added
  saturation taken back to 1.0; the lift is an expression that is zero before the set comes on (frame 29) and
  after it goes off (480), so the dead tube stays black.
- The user: "blacks look correct, theres a probably 1 to 2 pixel grey area, erode that in". Painting the matte
  solid red against the bare plate showed it: the luminance key stopped about 3 px inside the tube's dark
  edge, leaving a rim of the plate's static (67 of 255 beside a picture at 37). A BitmapMask's Border Width
  does nothing on a bitmap, so the key's source is dilated instead: `TubeGrow` (an ErodeDilate between
  `PlateSoft` and `TubeMatte`; X `0.0012*D`, Y `0.0005*D`, D the push's scale, since the rim is wider at the
  sides than top and bottom). The red test also showed a 25% ghost of the matte on the bezel's lit bottom lip,
  so the key is tighter, Low 0.545 and High 0.585 (0.57 to 0.64 opened holes in the tube's dim top corners).
  Checked on four corners at 8.4 s and whole frames at 2.5, 5.4 and 10.5 s: no holes, no ghost, the edge on
  the dark line within a pixel or so. One frame's static each; a played pass could still show a flicker.
- The switch-off, which the comp lacked (the picture cut to black under the letters). First built as rough
  5's: the last picture frame held, squashed to a 3 px line in 0.2 s while it brightens 2.2 times, the line
  dead by 20.5 s. Mid-build the user: "lets have clippa static out and then the line". Now the letters fill
  with static from 19.5 s (my pick, to keep the cutoff at 0:20; it replaces rough 5's last six one-frame
  flashes, some of them mostly black) and the static folds to the line at 20.0. Nodes: `StaticOut` (a
  TimeStretcher before `PicBulge`; from frame 468 it plays the screen clip's own static, its frames 41 up),
  then after `Bloom`: `OffHold` (holds frame 479), `OffFrame` (a SetDomain to the frame: without it the fold
  squashed the plate's out-of-frame static and bezel into view as bright bands), `OffFold` (Transform, Y size
  `max(3/1080, (1-e)^2)`), `OffHot` (gain, then the line's fade), `OffShut` (over black), into
  `BlackComesUp`. `LettersLift` fades with the fold and `PlainLetters` merges as Lighten so the hot line
  reads over the letters coming up. Checked on nine stills from 19:10 to 20:16, not played. The clips' sound
  on A1 still plays channel audio under the half second of static; no static hiss or switch-off sound yet.
- The user: "Length works", then "Fix the sound". The two stems are baked 25 s files (channel scraps and
  the opening's hiss in one; clicks, hum, a clunk or tick per channel and the off click and whine in the
  other), so both are re-cut: `mockups/demo-intro/work/sound6.py`, rough 5's sound code with the channels
  stopping at 19.5 s, a channel-change clunk there and hiss to 20 s (0.085 of white against the opening's
  0.06, since it follows loud channels). `--check` rebuilds the old stems first: identical to the kit's, to
  the sample. New files `stem_clips_24_v2.wav` and `stem_set_placeholder_24_v2.wav` in the kit (laptop and

  by board, so a per-pixel difference on the floor is mostly texture. `work/tv_share2.py` therefore takes the
  veil off (measured over the void), matches the boards and the cabinet's face each where the screen cannot
  reach them, and on the boards uses only the broad difference (both pictures blurred 40 px inside the floor
  matte, in front of the cabinet's foot only). Result, `kit/stage_tv_share_v2.png`: the screen is about 0.7 of
