## 2026-11-18 War room default layout

The user sent a full-page screenshot of the war room and asked to fix the dead space: with few items waiting the wide middle column was a short review queue under which nothing sat, while the left column held six panels (about 3,200 px against 1,100 in the middle). `DEFAULT_LAYOUT` in `clipper/web/js/app.js` now balances them: Pipeline and Modal left, the queue with Retakes, Health and API usage in the middle, the schedule with Costs on the right (previewed in the live tab, about 2021/1962/1595 px against 3238/1123/1316). A browser that already picked Layout > Default keeps its stored copy: click Default again. New test `test_default_layout_places_every_panel_once`; War-room wiki page says what the default is. The sharper case-study screenshot (the user takes it, 800 px is the most my pane captures) is still to do.

## 2026-11-18 War room redesign, step 2: pages and the top bar

The user asked for a better war room layout ("feel more like you want to use it", every function kept), step by step
with a UX/UI designer agent. Three rounds of questions settled it (memory `warroom-redesign`): an even mix of desk and
phone, review in bursts, opens on Review, Ctrl+K plus a few tabs, a docked side pane for items, four top-bar widgets
(upload cap, month spend, pipeline, health), chat hidden by default, fixed pages instead of the layout editor, a
Schedule with both a week calendar and a timeline, and a video-editor look with one alternate theme. A plan doc
(https://claude.ai/artifact/2Ny9Ad8bfWpjhyvtpx87eW, 8 steps; the user moved the page bar directly under the top bar
and asked for all eight pages on it) and a clickable mockup from a live snapshot
(https://claude.ai/artifact/H8uGqKzPmXJcXnAAUcNK4Z): "Looks good".

Step 2 on branch `warroom/pages`: the header is one row (live shows, Uploads / Spend / Pipeline widgets and a health
light that each open their page, Chat, ⋯ with Sync Kitsu, Replan, the log, the shortcuts); the page bar under it
shows Review, Schedule, Pipeline, Money, Stats, Library, Research and Settings, each one of today's panels or a group
of them; a phone keeps a bottom bar (Review, Schedule, Pipeline, Chat, More). Library, Research and Settings open
today's windows from tiles; the look is picked on Settings. The layout editor is gone (app.js zones,
`saved_layouts.py`, `/api/layouts`, `[paths] warroom_layouts`, its test); a new test checks the pages, the page bar
and the CSS agree. My code review found the queue keys still acting on the hidden queue from other pages (A could
approve an item you couldn't see), an empty aria-current, menus floating away on scroll, and the health light's level
read off its wording: fixed. Checked in the browser pane on the test server, desktop and a phone size.
Not deployed.

## 2026-11-18 War room redesign, step 3: the Review page

Step 2 (PR #314) merged and the user deployed it. Step 3 is PR #316: the item docks in a pane beside the Review grid
(about 40%, the left edge drags; below 1100 px, on a phone and from other pages it opens on top), J/K open and step it,
Esc closes it. Show, kind, status, order and dates went behind one Filters button with chips for what is on; cards
carry the show as a coloured tag (`--show-1..6` in every look, the overview's new `hue` from the show's place in
`[shows]`, so `hide_shows` moves no colour) and drop the "SHOW NAME |" prefix. The Retakes panel became queue tab 7;
an empty Waiting tab says "Nothing waiting" with the next upload; Undo moved under ⋯; the phone header fits 375 px.
My code review found 8 issues, all fixed; the worst was the item window opening invisibly (0x0, page inert) from
Schedule and Stats because the dialog now sat in the hidden Review page: it moves to `<body>` when on top. Others: tab
keys and `/` dead while docked, Space on a focused card playing the docked video, a saved pane width wider than a
smaller screen, the drag not ending on pointercancel, Esc order with the live menu, colours shifting with
`hide_shows`. Checked in the browser pane at 1600, 1000 and 375 px. The theme test caught `var(--show-${n})` in the
script, so the colour moved into `.show.s1`-`.s6` rules.

## 2026-11-18 War room redesign, step 4: the look

Step 3 (PR #316, notes #317) merged. Step 4 is PR #318, merged. The user picked `millennium` as the one alternate
look and bundled fonts. `themes/warroom.css` is a quiet editor palette (#111215 ground, #5b93f0 accent) with Inter
and JetBrains Mono in `clipper/web/fonts/` (their OFL licences beside them; `httpd.static_file` serves `.woff2` with a
month's `max-age`). Kinds became a dark badge with a coloured dot so colour on a card means the show; tighter header,
page bar, panels and dialogs; one accent focus ring. One line-icon SVG sprite in `warroom.html` and `ic(name)` in
core.js replace every emoji and symbol icon; a test checks each icon asked for is in the sprite and that no emoji or
old symbol is back. Panels show skeletons until the first overview, and a failed first load shows the error in them.
`retro` and `pager` are deleted; the chat page's default is now `millennium`.

My code review found five issues, all fixed: a kept pick of a retired look 404'd its stylesheet on every load (theme.js
now falls back on the stylesheet's error and forgets a pick serve doesn't list), the uploads skeleton pulsed forever on
a failed load, the Hold button's doubled gap, static icons not aria-hidden, and a symbol check too narrow to catch the
old arrows. Windows CI then failed a Kitsu deadline test unrelated to the look: `end - monotonic()` with the clock
unmoved rounded to 20.00000000000003 for a 20 s deadline, so the time left is capped at the deadline. Copilot didn't
review; the user asked for Grok ($0.64), which found one real bug: a click on an icon landed on its `<use>`, so Undo,
Select shown and the log's Refresh ignored icon clicks (handlers test `e.target.id`). `.ic` is now `pointer-events:
none`, with a test. Checked in the browser pane on the page-only preview. Not deployed.

## 2026-11-18 Versions: the stamp, v0.2.0, installing a release

The user asked whether a Go backend would help (no: the services are thin, the work is ffmpeg, Modal and model calls,
and the Python libraries and the shared modules are the point; the recorder is the only candidate, and only if it costs
something), then how the project is versioned. It wasn't: pyproject said 0.1.0, no tags, no releases, and nothing said
which commit the hub ran. Built (branch `release/version-stamp`): `clipper/buildinfo.py` (`0.2.0` on a tag,
`0.2.0+3.g570bb87` past it, `.dirty` with uncommitted changes, from `clipper/_build.txt` or `git describe`);
`deploy/stamp.sh`, which the hub and recorder deploys use to write the stamp into the staged copy since the image has no
`.git`; `clipper --version`, a doctor line and a `version` key in the serve, bot and recorder heartbeats, shown by
`clipper services status` next to this checkout's; the version bumped to 0.2.0 (pyproject, `__init__`, uv.lock) with the
changelog's Unreleased pile as its section; `.github/workflows/release.yml` (a `v*` tag makes a GitHub Release with the
wheel, sdist and the section's lead as notes, refusing a tag that disagrees with the files, has no lead or isn't on main); wiki Install
("A release on a new machine": clone, check out the newest tag, bootstrap), Developing#releases, NAS-hub. Not done: the
tag itself (the user's, after the merge), and no hub or recorder deploy (the user's). The first hub deploy after this
shows the version; until then the services' heartbeats have none and `services status` just omits it.
Then (same PR): the changelog had stopped at 11-16, so the 0.2.0 section would have left out the week's work; backfilled it from the merged PRs (checks and approval by silence, blind items, the messenger and its dock, title renames, Twitch and YouTube fetch, rejected keys, the war room's steps 2-3). The release notes are the section's lead (a sentence and highlights before its first `###`), not the whole diary; `release.yml` takes the lead and links the file. Documented in wiki Developing#changelog and CLAUDE.md (a bullet per user-visible PR, in that PR), with `tests/test_changelog.py` keeping the shape; `check.yml` runs on a CHANGELOG.md-only PR now, since a test reads it.

browser's address bar. Checked in the browser pane on the page-only preview. Not deployed.

## 2026-11-19 War room redesign, step 6: the Schedule's Next up, Week and tray

Step 5's notes (#323) merged and the hub deployed (steps 3-5 live). Step 6 is PR #329, merged. The Schedule page moved
out of core.js into `clipper/web/js/schedule.js`: Next up across the top (the next five going out, day/time and "in N h",
and the upload room left), then the Week or the List beside a sticky Ready to slot tray with Uploads under it. The Week
starts today (the plan's open question, picked by default), pages a week at a time out to `horizon_days`, and draws
each entry as a block in its hour (show colour, kind stripe; uploaded, planned and past differ), the open slots as dashed
drop targets, the day's count against `max_per_day`, and a red now line. The phone always gets the List, and the toggle
is hidden under 700 px. The tray shows exactly what the queue's Schedule tab counts; items that are stale or over budget
are one "N not going out" link to the queue's approved + dropped view. Server: `uploaded_at` on schedule entries,
`ended_at`/`release_at` per stream, open slots out to max(14, `horizon_days`).

My review found five, all fixed (a date formatter built per call, the not-going-out link with no guard if the page
didn't change, the stale items leaving the tray undocumented, the horizon computed twice, no tests). Grok's first run
gave an empty answer; the second ($0.18 in plus its thinking) found two: entries were still cut at 21 days while open
slots reached the horizon, so a taken slot past day 21 showed as open and a drop there would double-book it (fixed, with
a test), and "`goPage` is not defined" (wrong: palette.js has it, and the link was clicked in the browser pane). Checked
in the browser pane on live data, light look and millennium, desktop and 375 px. Not deployed.

## 2026-11-20 Analytics rides out Google's passing errors

The 00:30 analytics run (the deploy's restart starts every job) failed: the YouTube Analytics API answered
`500 backendError` to one video's 7-day query (puYkTaXKSH4), and `analytics.run` neither retried nor caught it, so the
traceback ended the run before `analytics.json` was written (the fresh view counts were lost too). The same query
answered three times in a row when asked by hand at once: passing. The channel-totals queries had hit the same 500 twice
on 11-19 (caught, but the run kept the old totals). Now every Analytics and Data API call passes `num_retries=5`
(googleapiclient's own backoff on 5xx, 429, rate-limit 403s and dropped connections); a video whose query still fails
with a passing error is skipped, its `d7_errors` counts the runs in a row, and the next run asks again; once one has
used up its retries the rest get one try each (an outage costs one backoff, not one a video); `analytics.json` is
written in a `finally`, so what came in counts even when a lasting error (a revoked sign-in) still raises. Only the same
video failing three runs in a row fails the job with a ClipperError, which serve DMs. My review's two left: retried
`videos.list` calls are counted once in the quota log (1 unit each), and serve waits a full interval after any failed
job (24 h for analytics); a retry-sooner option on `Job` would be its own change.
The first half (retries, skip, `finally`, tests) reached main inside the notes PR #335: a session sharing the checkout
committed what was staged here. PR #336 added the review's fixes (rate-limit 403s and DNS drops count as passing, one
backoff per outage, `youtube.RETRY_STATUS` reused) and this entry.
Grok on #336 ($0.62, the whole change against pre-#335 main) found two. One was real, fixed: after one video's lasting
500, `retries` stayed 0 for the rest of the run even once another video answered, so a passing error on a later video got
no retry and still counted toward GIVE_UP; a success now brings the retries back. The other was wrong: it said a
rate-limit 403's `error_details` is empty until `.reason` is read, but `HttpError.__init__` reads it. #336 merged on
green CI; the user deployed the hub. Its first analytics run (09:04) exited 0: 473 videos, 313 with 7-day numbers
(301 before), puYkTaXKSH4's among them.

## 2026-11-20 Chud overnight test ends

The scheduled wrap-up (`chud-overnight-wrapup`) checked the 71 chud segments pinned hourly 23:00-07:00 from 11-13 to
11-20: all uploaded, `publish_at` equal to the pin, no `last_error`, and the 69 YouTube still lists are public. The
other two, kick-136479997 s06 and s07 (Russell Brand, due 11-18 06:00 and 07:00), are uploaded in schedule.json but
`videos.list` no longer returns them (removed or deleted on YouTube); left as they are. Uploads per Pacific day were
15-26 (26 on 11-12), and the most in any rolling 24 h was 26: `max_per_24h = 26` held back uploads 47 times ("0 more
now") but nothing went past its slot. No `uploadLimitExceeded` or `quotaExceeded` in serve's logs all week; the busiest
quota day was 7,203 of 10,000 units. The only uploads after their slot were three asap pre-shows, a minute late as usual.
With the cap at 26 the backfills' reserve (26 x 600) left no spare quota on most days ("source links: no spare quota").

A replan simulated at `max_per_day = 15` moved nothing (no entry was left to upload). In `clipper.toml` (backup
`backups/clipper.toml.before_chud_overnight_end_2026-11-20`): `max_per_day` 24 -> 15, `max_per_24h` 26 -> 15, the
`[shows.chud.youtube] captions = false` table removed; `config.problems` empty, chud's captions on again. The 71 got
`captions_error = "deferred: chud overnight test 2026-11-12"` (backup `backups/schedule.json.before_chud_captions_2026-11-20`),
all with an .srt; the dry-run backfill lists all 71. At 400 units each (28,400) from each day's spare quota that is
about a week or two; the two missing videos will fail their three tries. Serve restarted on the hub (bot, worker,
recorder untouched). These notes went in with the analytics session's notes PR.

## 2026-11-20 serve retries a failed analytics or prune sooner

The follow-up the analytics fix left: a failed job waited its whole interval, 24 h for analytics. `Job` in serve.py gets
`retry` (default False); `interval(job, fails)` is `RETRY_FAILED_S` (30 min) doubled per failure in a row after the first,
never more than `every_s` (30 min, 1 h, 2 h, 4 h, 8 h, 16 h, then 24 h), and `due()`/`status()` use it. `run_clipper`
keeps `fails` in the job's state (+1 on a non-zero exit or a start that raised, 0 after exit 0); the heartbeat carries it
and `clipper services status` shows "N failed in a row" from 2. Marked retry: analytics and streams prune (free, safe to
repeat, a day apart). Not cast auto: each run can spend on Haiku labels and Brave searches. upload, tiktok, x, news and
blinds already run within the hour. The state is in memory, so a restart starts every job anyway. My review found two,
fixed: the HANDOFF line still called this unbuilt, and jobs_loop rebuilt the due set once per job.
Grok on #338 (about $0.07 in plus its thinking) found one, real, fixed: `auto_upload` runs `schedule` then `upload` under
the one job name, and each call moved the streak (both failing read "2 failed in a row"; a good `schedule` reset an
`upload` that failed every hour). `run_clipper(..., streak=False)` leaves it to the caller, and `count_run` counts the
cycle once. Upload isn't a retry job, so only the status line was wrong.

What carries over whatever the floor: stills are made whole in one pass, never joined from layers; the move is made from a clear-air plate and the haze is its own element over everything, tube picture included (`work/haze_dial.py` wrote a still one for the shag set; a moving one built in Fusion is the better end); light passes by edit are the same scene within 2 px, not exactly additive, so mix from the spot pass to the full picture on the set's on-curve; `work/track_take.py` tracks a take's tube; the still's utility passes come from the user's ComfyUI (they asked for "a traditional vfx pipeline" and allowed its downloads): `tv/aov/stage_{beauty,depth,matte_tube,matte_cabinet,matte_antenna,matte_floor,matte_worn_patch}.exr`, 32-bit, made by the workflow `clippa_aov_passes` in the ComfyUI app (Depth Anything V2 for depth, relative and soft-edged; Segment Anything 2.1 for tube, cabinet and floor; keys in a garbage box for the antenna and the worn patch, which the segmenter can't hold) or by `work/comfy_passes.py` against a running ComfyUI, sheet from `work/passes_sheet.py`, the workflow file from `work/comfy_ui_graph.py`. ComfyUI here is 0.44 under `AppData/Local/Comfy-Desktop/ComfyUI-Installs/ComfyUI`, venv and custom nodes in `Documents/ComfyUI`; never start the old copy under `AppData/Local/Programs/@comfyorgcomfyui-electron`, whose Manager downgrades numpy at startup (the log has the day's account and what was installed). The comp on take 4 is timeline `intro_v3_stage` (11-18), a copy of `intro_v2_comp`, which stays as the old set's: take 4 through a Loader as a PNG sequence (`kit/plate_take4_seq/` on the NAS share), the path refitted (`work/fit_take4.py`: the approved push with only a 1.25x blow-up at the end where the old one had 1.78x), the key at 0.62-0.70 with the still's SAM tube matte filling its inside (`TubeFill`; take 4's static is dark in the tube's top corners), the spot on at frame 8 before the set at 1.2 s (`LightMix`: the plate lit by spot x (1 - share) + screen light x share, the share measured from a light pass since 11-18, `kit/stage_tv_share_v2.png`, see below), the dead tube as dark grey glass until the set comes on, the haze as its own element over everything (`HazeAdd`; `HazeLevel.Gain` 0.12 turns it), and the spot's switch on A3 (a generated sound since 11-18, `kit/spot_on_cue_24_v2.wav`). A render of it is `mockups/demo-intro/renders/intro_v3_stage_preview.mp4` (with a 720p copy; 45 minutes to render, about 4.5 s a frame), checked on frames by `work/check_render.py`. The drawn map and the stand-in cue are kept beside them (`kit/stage_tv_share.png`, `kit/spot_on_cue_24.wav`): `TVShareFile`'s clip and the A3 clip are the two things to point back. After that render, on the user's notes (11-18): the tube's matte is blurred to a 3 px edge (`TubeEdgeSoft`, Blur 1.0 on `TubeFill`, feeding `Merge2`; a Fusion blur of 2.5 is an 8 px edge) and a light wrap puts the picture's light on the bezel for about 10 px (`WrapScreen` > `WrapSoft` 12 > `WrapGain` 0.5 > `Wrap`, added outside the matte, between `Merge2` and `Bloom`). The key's shape itself wobbled 1-3 px inside the tube's blue rim and wandered along the bottom (stills in `renders/matte_check/`, made by `work/matte_sheet.py`), so the user drew a roto shape: `TubeRoto`, a 13-point polygon keyed once on comp frame 201 over the unpushed plate (`Merge1`), pinned to the track by its Center and Size expressions (the tube's path times the tube's size over its size on that frame). Three notes on that ending are in too: the collapsing band is motion blurred (`OffFold` and `OffBand`, shutter 300, quality 10), the dark line it left across the letters for four frames is gone (the white has no hole after the fold, and `LineMax`, a Custom tool, takes the lighter of the white and the line, so the line can only brighten), and the final letters are brighter (`PlainWhite` times 1.085: 252 of 255 in the render, was 230).     - Stream-copy cuts must start on a keyframe (every ~4 s) or audio drifts; `.ts` seeking for re-encodes needs
      keyframe seek + output trim. ffmpeg backs input seeks off by 3/23 s when video has B-frames: `.ts` lands on
      the keyframe at/after the target, `.mkv` (NAS recorder) at/before it, so `copy_cut` seeks k+0.005 for .ts
      and k+0.15 otherwise, and fails the cut on a sync gap or wrong length. Each compilation part ends with a
      concat `outpoint` where the next file begins, or joins replay 1-5 s (the H3 video, 10-28).

## 2026-11-22 split_over_s: negative is off, text is an error

  cut by min_s/max_s and nothing is flagged for a re-split. A value float() cannot read, such as
  `fifty`, or a bool, raises ClipperError naming the setting and the value, instead of a bare
  TypeError on the comparison. Production clipper.toml is untouched.

## 2026-11-22 Blind items: custody of the store (branch blinds/custody)

- The user: "focus on getting the data and being a proper custodian". Found: the blinds store (`blinds/blinds.sqlite`, your answers
  and the reveals) was in no backup, and `prune` forgot every blind older than `keep_days` (365) except answered ones, so reveals
  (the ground truth) and "no idea" answers were lost after a year.
- Built: `clippa backup` copies the store in as a snapshot (SQLite backup API, integrity_check, stops on a failed check); prune
  keeps answered, no-idea and reveal rows. The backup module joins the list of modules allowed to read the store (a private zip).
- Opus re-check (same day): a damaged store raised and held up the whole taste backup (now it goes in as
  `blinds.damaged.sqlite` with a warning line); an edited post overwrote the text the clues were read from (now its old text
  goes into history, in one UPDATE with json_insert so a concurrent answer isn't clobbered); the defaults comment was stale.
  The first answer was wrong twice: there is no 0-100 scorer and no `blinds calibrate` yet, both are #111's design.
- The real store (snapshot, read-only on the source): 706 blinds since 10-17 (2 answered), 198 reveals, 5 edited posts,
  no pairs. 105 blinds' opening 60 characters appear verbatim in a reveal: step 4 can match in code first.
- Not done: matching reveals to their blinds (step 4), and the doctor check for a stale store or a missing backup of it.
- Step 4 part 1 (user: "Start step 4"): reveals are a date line, the blind's text and one answer line (all 106 matched ones
  had exactly one). `match_reveals` runs in every check, code only: word 3-gram overlap >= 0.6 and 0.3 ahead of the next blind
  within a day of the date line; a garbled date compares every earlier blind. Five columns added by connect() (revealed,
  reveal_id, revealed_at on a blind; reveal_of, reveal_score on a reveal). `grade` scores your guess (right / wrong / late,
  late = guessed after the reveal was posted); `clippa blinds reveals` prints the scorecard; `list` marks [revealed] without the
  name. Prune keeps revealed blinds. On a copy of the real store: 106 matched, 0 doubles, the two lowest scores (0.81, 0.94)
  checked by eye and right; 90 wait for older blinds, 2 garbled. Discord follow-up, war room, calibrate: not built.
- Reviews: the local code-review (7 findings) and Grok at 349270b (6) all fixed before the merge; Grok at 54eb248 posted two more
  just before the user merged #384 (a busy store left out of a backup still let rotation delete the zip that held it; `show` and
  `reveals` raised when people.json couldn't be read). Both real, fixed in a follow-up PR with tests.
- Backfill (user: "run the backfill on the older reveals", then "rest of 2026"), in the serve container over ssh so SQLite writes
  stay off SMB, `--no-clues`, a backup before each: 90 days brought 1,060 posts, 326 days (`CLIPPER_BLINDS_BACKFILL_PAGES=80`)
  4,180 more: 6,144 back to 2026-01-01. The new code on a copy matches 1,572 of 1,852 reveals, 0 doubles, lowest score 0.82. Of
  the rest, 193 are for blinds before 2026, 48 quote a blind that is nowhere in the store, 35 are "Revealed <date>" posts of
  2012-13 blinds; a wider date window found 1 more, so it stays at a day. Stopped at 2026: the deployed prune (365 days) would
  delete older backfilled blinds before the deploy.

## 2026-11-23 Review check: missing OpenCV is not a bad cover

- `clippa review check` was catching `No module named 'cv2'` per item and saving it as a cover
  finding with a matching signature, so the next run skipped those items (266 bad covers on a
  broken install). It now imports cv2 only for an item that would actually call it, checks
  the others first and saves them, then raises ClipperError without writing that failure
  into checks.json. A result already saved from that failure is checked again.
  OpenCV stays in the `faces` extra (`opencv-python-headless`); not a missing dependency.

## 2026-11-23 OpenRouter Claude overlay (not applied)

  the production overrides, which use the same bare names) becomes `openrouter:anthropic/claude-*.5`
  at the same tier. Effort keys are not in the file, and neither is `[llm.prices]`. A `--dry-run`
  (`estimate_lines`) and a call that omits its cost use `known_price`: the table, then OpenRouter's
  public `/models` list. A failed fetch is cached for the process, so later rows count $0 without
  waiting again. Needs `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`. Defaults stay on Claude. Production `clipper.toml`
  was not edited. The three catalog ids are on OpenRouter's public model list; the hyphen form is not.

## 2026-11-23 OpenRouter spending limits stop a stage

- `openrouter_key_limit` (HTTP 402 or 403) and `openrouter_credits` (HTTP 402) raise
  `OpenRouterLimit` and are not sent again. `openrouter_in_flight_budget` waits for
  `Retry-After` (capped at 60 s) and retries, up to the client's six attempts, then the same
  error. A stop during segments writes nothing: no `segments.json` on a first run (a previous
  one is left byte for byte), no checkpoint, no cut, no temp file. The worker still retries a
  failed run on its usual schedule; this is not a rejected key. Production `clipper.toml` was
  not touched.

## 2026-11-24 OpenRouter budget panel

- Money page gains an OpenRouter budget panel. API usage stays. The inference key is read
  server-side (`OPENROUTER_API_KEY` or `secrets/openrouter_secret.txt`). A management key
  (`OPENROUTER_MANAGEMENT_KEY` or `secrets/openrouter_management_secret.txt`) can change the
  limit or disable the key after a confirm; without it the panel is read-only. The warning
  percent defaults to 75 and is saved in `streams/.serve/openrouter.json`. Spend turns amber
  past the warning and red when the cap is spent; the health light says Stopped for
  `openrouter_key_limit`. A 402 with Retry-After is `openrouter_in_flight_budget` and is retried.
  Banner sentences live in `clippa/openrouter_budget.py` `COPY`, for when a real 402 shows what
  was left on disk. Production `clipper.toml` was not touched. Draft PR, not merged, not deployed.

  band-aid scoped to workers from before #431. What stays open: stream to a Modal Volume, re-run

  tests, not named in the ticket.

  a wiki, and a wrong `exited -15` boundary (it was the 11-24 message too); fixed, and the same
  boundary corrected in Transcribe-on-Modal. Notes only: no CHANGELOG bullet (Developing#changelog

## 2026-11-25 A VOD whose timeline jumps back: the transcribe check and `fetch --fixup` (#440)

- `kick-132975332` (Chud 10-18) has PTS 62.0 -> 883.8, then back to 62.0 and on to 33073: one backwards
  step in both the video and the audio. Container 33011.2 s against 33833.0 s of audio, so a transcript
  from it is 821.8 s late after the jump, and every cut and Short would point at the wrong moment.
- The duration-overlap probe an issue proposed cannot see it: the second stretch starts at the file's own
  start (measured 0.000 overlap). The user chose detection at transcribe instead: after `extract_audio`,
  `check_audio_timeline` compares `audio.wav` with the source container (real: raises 821.8 s naming the
  stream and `clippa fetch <url> --fixup`). Same check in `speakers --diarize` and in `archive transcribe`
  before it drops the item's source.
- `clippa fetch <url> --fixup` (Kick only, `.ts` only): `-fflags +genpts -c copy -f mpegts` into a fresh
  `mkstemp` `.timeline-*.part` beside the source (never matched by `source.*`), then three checks before it
  replaces the original: the probed length no shorter, packet order on both streams (any step back > 0.5 s
  is a failure, a missing PTS fails closed), and at least 90% of the source's bytes. `--fflags +genpts` is
  not itself the fix (ffmpeg's muxer relinearises the non-monotonic DTS); the checks are what prove it. The
  temp goes on every exit; the run lock is held only for the busy check and the rename; `timeline_overlap_s`
  lands in meta.json when the jump is over 1 s. Both tools get `-protocol_whitelist file`, so a hostile
  `source.ts` can't send them to a url.
- Proven on copies of the real file: two byte-concatenated 90 s excerpts (90.2 s container, 180 s content)
  remux to 180.23 s with the packet count kept; a truncated copy is refused. Not yet run on the real
  stream: `clippa fetch <url> --fixup` then re-transcribe it reads the file three times when it jumps (about
  100 GB of I/O) and rewrites 33 GB. `jumps_back` alone takes ~145 s warm on that file.
- The repair leaves no stale audio behind: `_remux_guarded` drops `audio.wav` inside the same locked
  section as the replace (a touched wav decoded from the old source would pass the length check — after a
  repair the two lengths agree within a second — and carry the wrong sample positions), and `extract_audio`
  also re-decodes a wav older than its source by more than the 2 s FAT/SMB round timestamps by, which covers
  a killed fixup and a hand-replaced file. The comparison is between the two files' own times, never this
  clock: a source stamped in the future (SMB/FAT skew) is no exemption, so a stale wav can't ride on one.
  Taken deliberately, with its cost: such a source re-decodes on every call, because a wav written "now"
  can never look newer than it. Rare and visible; the alternative was reusing audio decoded from a
  different file, which is the one outcome this check exists to stop.
- Grok's review of the PR found two things the internal rounds had not: the archive path deleted the only
  complete copy on the next run (`_drop_partial` unlinks `source.ts`, then a Kick re-download from an
  unrefreshed m3u8 can fail), and `clippa fetch --fixup` could not repair anything when Kick's api failed
  because `resolve` ran first. Now a refused download is renamed `source.refused.ts` (matched by
  `source_file`, not by `_drop_partial`, sorts before `source.ts` so it cannot be bypassed, after
  `source.mp4` so a kept video wins) and reused without downloading; with `--fixup`, a resolve failure
  falls back to the local folder found by uuid (`_stream_for_vod`), and two folders holding the same video
  is an error, never a guess.
- Then the follow-up rounds: a repair within tolerance returned 0.0 and left the wav (measured on real
  media); the archive message spliced the guard's `clippa fetch` url into `error.txt` (now
  `TimelineJump.reason`); a link at `source.refused.ts` made every run re-download 33 GB; and
  `is_symlink()` is false for an NTFS junction, which needs no privilege — `_is_link` covers both, a linked
  wav is dropped, a linked source is refused, a linked or out-of-tree folder is never repaired, a failed
  decode is a one-line `ClipperError` rather than a `CalledProcessError` traceback and leaves no partial
  `audio.tmp.wav`, and a junction (a folder to `unlink`, which Windows refuses at either spot) is dropped
  with `rmdir`, the link alone.
- Tests: 1697 passed, 23 skipped (offline; ffprobe/ffmpeg mocked; ten symlink tests skip on this box for
  lack of the privilege and run on Linux CI, the four junction tests do run here). Reviews: correctness
  caught that the proposed probe was blind, then that the remux proof was missing and the stale wav;
  security the temp readable as the source, the forgeable check, the url-openable tools and the links; tests
  six tests that would have passed with their guard deleted. A `fetch_video` C901 claim was withdrawn after
  `ruff --select C901` disagreed with the reviewer's count. Filed #446 (an archive item's kept source has
  no repair path) and #447 (whitelist the other stages' ffmpeg calls).

## 2026-11-26 Ten review rounds on #440: what the PR guarantees now

- The PR was up with Grok reviewing each push; ten more passes found fourteen real edges, all one
  class: never lose the only complete copy, never read another file's audio. Fixed and tested
  here: a corrupt `meta.json` turning the timeline refusal into a `ValueError` (skipping the archive
  quarantine); a fast `--fixup` leaving a stale `audio.wav` the length guard cannot see (now an
  `audio.wav.stale` marker written before the replace, honoured whatever the mtime, and its decode
  against a source that changed under it refused); a shadowing `source.mp4` dropped only after the
  download (now before it too, so a refused drop costs no 33 GB); only `TimelineJump` quarantined, so
  a read failure retries next run; a finished download marked `.refused` when a post-download drop or
  stale mark fails; pathlib re-raising EACCES (a name a share won't stat is not an absent one, and
  archive's drops say "couldn't tell" in one line); every download marking the wav stale (`fetch`
  and `archive transcribe`, since yt-dlp stamps Last-Modified); `_merge_listing` keeping an item whose
  `source.mp4` won't stat; `_stream_for_vod` skipping an unstattable entry.
- Pushed back as pre-existing and already filed: the archive flac encode's `-y` and missing
  whitelist (#447), and a refused archive source having no repair path (#446).
- Filed #448 (an `audio.wav.src` stamp beside the wav, and an in-order `--fixup` clearing a wav the
  guard calls stale) and #449 (`is_link` on a share that won't stat is a traceback from
  `fetch`/`fixup`, not one line).
- Tests 1753 passed / 39 skipped; ruff clean; mkdocs strict green in CI. Merged as PR #444;
  `kick-132975332` still waits for `clippa fetch <url> --fixup` (three reads of 33 GB and one rewrite).

## 2026-11-26 #448: audio.wav says which source it was decoded from

- `extract_audio` writes `audio.wav.src` (the source's `st_size` and `st_mtime_ns`, from the lstat the
  decode began with) after it installs a wav, and reuses a cached wav only when that stamp still names
  the source: size exactly, mtime within `MTIME_ROUNDING_S` (the laptop over SMB and a container on the
  NAS read one file's time at different precisions, so an exact match would re-decode 33 GB for nothing).
  Order: the stale marker first, then the stamp, then (no stamp: a wav from before this) the old mtime
  rule. A stamp that is a link, isn't JSON or isn't `{size: int, mtime_ns: int}` is a no, not an absence.
  The stamp goes with its wav (`forget_stamp`: extract_audio's drop, kick's `_drop_wav`, archive after the
  flac encode).
- `archive transcribe` passes `trust_mtime=False`: an item's wav is only a failed run's leftover, so one
  with no stamp is decoded again (closes the `.refused` reuse case from the 321e36f review).
- `kick._remux_guarded`: when nothing was replaced (the source already in order) and the wav runs past the
  container by more than `timeline.TOLERANCE_S` (`transcribe.audio_lengths`, the guard's own numbers,
  now shared with `check_audio_timeline`), it marks the wav stale and drops it under the run lock, with a
  line. The replace path's drop moved into `_drop_wav`.
- Tests: the stamp's record, a mismatch with a newer wav, rounding across precisions, a matching stamp
  over the mtime rule, the marker over a matching stamp, broken and linked stamps, archive's no-stamp
  decode, the stamp going with a stale wav and a source changed mid-decode; three in-order fixup cases.
  `FakeFf` answers `format=duration` with `wav_s`.

## 2026-11-25 Deploys refuse from a checkout behind main

- Minutes after #437 was deployed, the claude container was redeployed from a checkout without it
  (`C:\clipper` on a feature branch): the image lost nodejs while `home/` kept the Hindsight hooks,
  and every hook said `node: not found` until a redeploy from main. Nothing enforced "deploy from main".
- `deploy/mainguard.sh` (`require_main`): fetch `origin/main`, refuse (exit 1) when HEAD is behind it,
  naming the branch, commit and count; `--force` overrides; offline it checks the last fetched main
  and says so; commits ahead of main and uncommitted changes are only noted. Wired into
  `deploy/hub/deploy.sh` and `deploy/claude/deploy.sh`, both of which also take `--env PATH`
  (env.local is gitignored and lives in `C:\clipper`), so a worktree of main can deploy.
- Tested by hand: behind main both refuse, `--force` goes on, up to date they reach ssh (a bogus
  host, no real deploy). A pytest covers it on Linux. Wiki: Claude-on-the-NAS#deploy, NAS-hub#deploy,
  Bugs-to-remember.
- Then the agent and recorder deploys too. The recorder's override is `--any-branch`, because its
  `--force` already means "while a show is live" and Bugs-to-remember forbids it; neither flag skips
  the other check. Its live check also finds `clippa` on PATH or in the main checkout's `.venv`, so it
  runs from a worktree of main. Tested by hand: behind main, record refuses with and without `--force`,
  `--any-branch` passes and runs `record status`; agent refuses behind, passes up to date.

## 2026-11-26 #449, #447 and #446: one-line link checks, local-only ffmpeg, `archive fixup`

- #449 (PR #451): `media.link_or_raise(p, label, what)` turns an `OSError` from `is_link` (Python 3.12
  re-raises EACCES from `is_symlink`/`isjunction`) into one `ClipperError` line naming the stream; used by
  kick's and cli's stream-folder and source checks, `transcribe`'s and archive's `_drop_link`. A refused
  stat of `source.ts.ytdl` says it couldn't tell whether the source is still downloading.
- #447 (PR #452): `-protocol_whitelist file` before each local `-i` across the stages (lavfi inputs exempt,
  b-roll's url fetches through `broll.src_opts`); `media.drop_output_link` removes a link at an output name
  first. `tests/test_ffmpeg_whitelist.py` scans the AST for ffmpeg/ffprobe argv lists; the allow-list
  names modal's url transcribe, b-roll's url probes and the recorder's join (bookworm ffmpeg 5.1, untried).
- #446: `clippa archive fixup <subject> <item> [--force]` (`archive.fixup_item`). Decisions (user): its own
  command, not a `transcribe` flag; HEI the same as Kick (repair the kept copy, no download); a kept
  `source.mp4` is named in the refusal and never remuxed. The remux replaces `source.refused.ts` (or the
  marked `source.ts`) in place: renamed back to `source.ts` it would be unlinked as a partial. Order as in
  kick: `mark_stale` before `os.replace`, then `transcribe.drop_wav` (moved from kick's `_drop_wav`). Unlike
  a stream's in-order `--fixup`, an in-order copy is refused with its wav kept (the wav came from the same
  bytes, so a drop only buys a decode refused again), and a copy whose size or mtime changed during the
  remux is not replaced. Refuses while `services.recorder` says live or can't tell. The card's preview
  reads through `archive.set_aside` (read-only), not `item_source`, which tidies the folder. Left as
  decided in #444: a set-aside copy stays after its transcript (a run drops only what it downloaded). Agent-first (#365): a `Tool("archive_fixup", SPEND)` whose card says "no model or Modal cost; NAS
  disk time only" and runs `archive fixup -- <subject> <item>`; the chat answers in text for a kept video,
  nothing to repair, an unknown or traversing id, or a live show. The refusal lines in `transcribe_item`
  name the command (`_kept_note`). Tests in `tests/test_archive_fixup.py`.
- Ran the repair on `kick-132975332` after the 11-26 show: `fetch --fixup` (Kick's api couldn't resolve the
  url, so the copy here was repaired) found the 821.8 s jump and remuxed it, 33011.2 s -> 33833.0 s. The plain
  `transcribe` refused: its `words.jsonl` was from before #440's check. `transcribe --force` on Modal L40S,
  ~$0.37, 78,623 words, timeline check passed. Hub redeployed from main (f5e38d4) after. So a `--fixup` that
  rewrites a source with a transcript now prints `clippa transcribe <id> --force` (in PR #453).
- Grok on #453 at 8731f34, three findings: (1) the kept-video line said to delete `source.mp4`, which may be
  the only copy (a dead link is then skipped, a Kick m3u8 is never refreshed): now `archive.kept_advice` says
  move it out of the folder and keep it, or names the fixup when a `.ts` is set aside beside it. (2) `.refused`
  also marks a download whose stale mark or shadow drop failed, so an in-order copy was called unfixable:
  `_in_order_note` measures the wav first and sends a fine copy to `archive transcribe`. (3) "a share error in
  put_in_place is a traceback": not so, `remux_verified` wraps its OSError into one line; no change.
- Grok on #453 at e1e3821: (1) an `archive fixup` landing between `extract_audio` reusing the old wav and the
  timeline check makes the old wav and new container agree, so the check passes and the item is done from
  jumping audio: `transcribe_item` now compares the source's (size, mtime) before the read with after the
  check (`_refuse_if_replaced`) and transcribes nothing on a change. (2) An overrunning wav was called final
  even when the next run would decode it again: `transcribe.reuses_wav` (extract_audio's own rule, split out
  as `_wav_is_for`) decides, so only a wav whose stamp names this copy makes it "can't fix". (3) The
  put_in_place traceback again: still wrapped by `remux_verified`; no change.

## 2026-11-27 (evening): #456 closed, #454 the OpenRouter fallback, stopgap removed

- #456 (the fresh download deleted before the encode) was already fixed by PR #455 on 11-26, which
  never linked it. Checked each point of its checklist against `clippa/archive.py` and its tests,
  then closed it with a comment pointing at #455 (user's word).
- #454, PR #463: `[llm] fallback` (`""` default, or `"openrouter"`). A bare `claude-*` call that
  Anthropic refuses for credits or billing (402, or a 400/403 saying "credit balance"/"billing")
  is resent as `openrouter:anthropic/claude-*-N.N`, if OpenRouter lists that id. The process then
  keeps using OpenRouter for an hour (shared across clients) before trying Anthropic again. An auth
  error still raises `KeyRejected` and never falls back. With the fallback off, the model isn't
  listed, or there's no OpenRouter key, it raises `OutOfCredits` (exit 5). The worker holds the stream
  (run.json `out_of_credits`, retried when the keys change or after `retry_minutes`) and the alerts DM
  says so once (key `out_of_credits`). Serve does the same for its own jobs. `stops_run()` replaces
  `key_rejected or OpenRouterLimit` in meta, retake, review_check and thumbs (thumbs no longer
  swallows it). Checkup, repairs, `keys.held()` and doctor (`check_llm_fallback`) know about it. Wiki:
  Model-providers "When Anthropic is out of credits", How-the-processes-talk (exit 5). 15 tests in
  `tests/test_llm_fallback.py`.
- The local high-effort review found 6. Fixed: thumbs swallowing the error, `keys.held()` missing credit
  holds, and the notice printing once per process forever (now once per fallback period). Skipped:
  serve's INFO lines and a test of serve's exit-5 path. Grok's review: no correctness bugs ($0.89).
  CI green, merged 18:53 PT (a60254a) on the user's word.
- The user deployed the hub. Serve and the bot report `0.4.0+118.g85239e6`. Then the 11-26 stopgap
  came out of the NAS `clipper.toml`: backed up to `backups/clipper.toml.before_stopgap_removal_2026-11-27`,
  rebuilt from `before_openrouter_stopgap_2026-11-26` with this morning's `[segments] max_s = 5400`
  carried over (the only other change since), plus `[llm] fallback = "openrouter"`. Checked: it loads,
  no stopgap model is left, the OpenRouter key is found, and no stream is held. `[agent] model` was
  `openrouter:` before the stopgap and stays.
- Branch cleanup (user's word): removed 9 worktrees (all merged and clean, and none used by an open
  session), 38 local branches merged into main, and 56 merged branches on origin, including four Cursor branches
  whose PRs were squash-merged. Kept: `main`, the four kept on purpose (instagram-source, story-dig, jev-lab,
